A Darwinian individual is a reproducer with high reproductive causal control, meaning heredity survives as the parent's description is made finer. On that vocabulary, genes of multicellular organisms are, like hearts, mere reproducees, but with high control. Material overlap is necessary for reproducers, which are whole life cycles, and unnecessary for reproducees, so Griesemer and Godfrey-Smith partly talk past each other. Both quantities get an information-theoretic recipe (conditional MI after Bertschinger et al. 2008 for autonomy; interventional MI across grains, Griffiths et al.'s INF, for control), and neither is computed. | Read |
| [NOTE-101](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-101.md) v2 | Luque — Philosophical analysis of the Price equation | Luque argues that the Price equation, Δz̄ = Cov(w, z) + E(wΔz) (eq. 1, p. 7), is a Kuhnian "generalization-sketch", like f = ma. It is not a model, has no predictive power on its own, and becomes empirical only when a specific model interprets it. On that reading the critics' charges (van Veelen 2005; van Veelen et al. 2012; Nowak & Highfield 2011) that it is "a tautology" and "lacks predictive power" are either false or ask too much, and he recommends that evolutionary biologists adopt it as their unifying framework. | Read |
| [NOTE-102](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-102.md) v2 | Bottou & Schölkopf — Borges and AI | A short essay, not an argument with evidence. It claims that a "perfect language model", defined as sampling each next word from a random occurrence of the current text in the infinite collection of all plausible texts, is a fiction machine governed by "narrative necessity", to which "neither truth nor intention" matters. On that view hallucinations "are just confabulations", chat-bot answers take the role the user's framing implies, and fine-tuning and RLHF are attempts to prune the garden "against its nature". It warns that power over what LLMs write becomes "power over what we think". | Read |
| [NOTE-103](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-103.md) v2 | SEP — Philosophy of statistical mechanics | The entry argues that statistical mechanics has no canonical formalism but three umbrellas — Boltzmannian SM, the Boltzmann Equation and Gibbsian SM — and that every live account of the approach to equilibrium (ergodic, typicality, Mentaculus, long-run residence time, coarse-graining, interventionist, epistemic) survives Loschmidt's and Zermelo's objections only by giving up strict irreversibility or adding something outside the isolated dynamics; it closes on the unresolved "schism" that physicists compute in GSM while philosophers explain in BSM. | Read |
| [NOTE-104](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-104.md) v2 | Schwitzgebel — AI and Consciousness | A book-length sceptical argument that neither introspection, conceptual analysis nor any current theory (GWT, HOT, IIT, local recurrence, unlimited associative learning, biological views) can tell us whether near-future AI is conscious. The best sceptical argument, Mimicry, only undercuts the inference from humanlike text to consciousness and does not show absence. On the Leapfrog Hypothesis the first disputably conscious AIs will be person-like, and the question will then be settled socially rather than scientifically (the "Social Semi-Solution"). | Read |
| [NOTE-105](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-105.md) v2 | Dorato — Events and the Ontology of Quantum Mechanics | Dorato argues, without formal results, that events are the metaphysical category best suited to quantum mechanics. An event reading fits GRW flashes, GRW matter density and Bohmian worldlines, and also the antirealist views of ψ held by Bohr, Rovelli, Haag and Pashby. Because it is the same across interpretations, he calls it "interpretation-independent" (p. 12). Where ψ needs a realist reading, it should be read as intrinsic dispositions of events. Esfeld et al.'s view has the opposite structure: bare matter points plus a single holistic power of the universal configuration. Dorato argues that view is unintelligible, and that it must change its ontology when it passes from the universal wave function to the effective one (pp. 13–15). | Read |
| [NOTE-106](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-106.md) v2 | Angius et al. — Making sense of transformer success | Current attempts to explain transformer language models' competence fall into three kinds, and the paper maps each onto an explanatory form from philosophy of cognitive science: machine psychology is Cummins-style functional analysis, induction-head work is Machamer–Darden–Craver mechanistic explanation, and NLM–fMRI brain-score studies are a bidirectional "co-simulation". Its only thesis is that these do not differ from how cognitive science explains humans. Functional analyses are to be reduced to mechanisms as "mechanism sketches". The paper rests on existing literature and adds no new analysis or data. | Read |
| [NOTE-107](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-107.md) v2 | Religion and Science (SEP) | A survey, not an argument. It reports that the scholarly field rejects the conflict model the public holds, and it finds Barbour's four-way taxonomy (conflict, independence, dialogue, integration) influential but neither exhaustive nor exclusive. Its survey of five traditions shows the relation varies with creed and colonial history, and it describes the field fragmenting into claim-by-claim "science engaged theology". Its metaphysical content is concentrated in §1.2 (methodological vs ontological naturalism) and §3.1 (divine action under deterministic, chaotic and quantum physics; primary vs secondary causation; chance and providence). | Read |
| [NOTE-108](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-108.md) v2 | Søgaard — Is unsupervised clustering somehow truer? | Søgaard rebuts two arguments that unsupervised clustering is epistemically superior to supervised classification. The No-Bias Argument fails because clustering is also sensitive to sample bias (a toy example moves the boundary from x = 7 to x = 6), and it is more sensitive to representation bias. The Simplicity-Truth Argument fails because clustering is also function estimation and overfits in the same way (Hansen & Larsen 1996). From the fact that every clustering hypothesis is in a supervised classifier's hypothesis class, he concludes that supervised classification is "at least as justified", but that positive step is under-argued. | Read |
| [NOTE-109](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-109.md) v2 | Bender et al. — What Lives? (definitions of life) | Three LLMs (Claude 3.7 Sonnet, GPT-4o, Llama 3.3 70B), each run 3 times on both orderings of every pair, rated 68 hand-solicited definitions of life on a −1 to 1 "correlation" rubric. The averaged matrix, cut by complete-linkage clustering at an elbow, gives 8 clusters, 45/68 of them (66%) in "Cognitive Autonomy" (n=24) and "Dissipative Self-Organizing Systems" (n=21). The clusters are weak: the repo's own saved statistics give an overall silhouette of 0.079, and cross-model agreement is an unadjusted Rand index of 71–79% against a chance level of about 62–74%. The headline that life is "a continuous semantic landscape" is not tested against any alternative. | Read |
| [NOTE-110](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-110.md) v2 | Belot — Quantum states for primitive ontologists | Inside primitive-ontology Bohmian mechanics, Belot compares four readings of the quantum state: a field on configuration space, a multi-field on 3-space, a law, and a history of velocity dispositions of the particle system. Every reading except the multi-field carries a hard open question: why the world looks 3-dimensional, whether laws can be time-indexed, and whether dispositions lose content. The last is shown by the particle in a box, where every energy eigenstate φn = sin(πnx) induces the same at-rest disposition. The orthodox should prefer the multi-field reading; Belot, on van Fraassen's view of interpretation, prefers to keep the others alive. | Read |
| [NOTE-111](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-111.md) v2 | Baedke et al. — Unknotting reciprocal causation | The paper gives an epistemic, not a metaphysical, tool for organism–environment reciprocal causation. The loop is "unrolled" into two ordered series of states, O_n and E_n, joined by interventionist (Woodward) causal arrows, and an experienced-environment node Ex_n is added as the interface. Its authors argue that this one diagram keeps an epistemically set organism–environment boundary and maps constitutive, mediational, relational, relocational and physical niche construction onto shared causal patterns. They support this with worked cases, including two multi-species ones (bumblebee–eggplant; rumen microbes–ruminant host). | Read |
| [NOTE-112](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-112.md) v2 | Poltorak — Wave-function realism and causal closure | A closure principle has two tacit parameters, its domain and its completeness relation (causal, dynamical or explanatory). Wave-function realism falsifies spatiotemporal-domain closure (STC) but is compatible with fundamental-physical closure (FPC), so there is no "trans-domain causal breach". The physicalist is left with a dilemma: STC is too narrow, and FPC needs an independent criterion of physicality plus a dynamical-to-causal bridge. The paper tests this across primitive-ontology, spacetime-state, multi-field, Everettian, nomological and dispositional readings. | Read |
| [NOTE-113](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-113.md) v2 | Status distrust of scientific experts | Desmond gives a "how-possibly" argument, not a demonstration, that stubborn distrust of scientific experts can be valid reasoning. A low-status truster who is vulnerable in collective decision-making (Premise 1) and has reason to think the prevailing "service-for-prestige" ethos leaves out their core interests (Premise 2) has reason to distrust a high-prestige expert because of that prestige (§6, p. 16–17). The paper explicitly declines to say when those premises are true. | Read |
| [NOTE-114](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-114.md) v2 | SEP — Biological individuals | A survey that maps biological individuals by crossing two axes: organism / some parts of organisms / some groups of organisms, against living agent (physiological) / Darwinian (evolutionary) individual. The result is nine regions, each given one example (fruit fly, gene, eusocial colony, lysosome, clade, heart, coral reef, virus, squid+Vibrio). "Part" is never defined, and the entry gives no criterion for *which* parts or groups count: the placements are the authors' tentative judgements, "moving from less contentious to more contentious examples" (§7). | Read |
| [NOTE-115](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-115.md) v2 | Meincke — A process view of pregnancy | Meincke argues that substance ontology forces a binary count of individuals. The Containment View fails empirically, following Kingma. Kingma's Parthood View either loses foster–neonate identity ("Parts-of-Substances") or cannot count consistently ("Substances-as-Parts"). If organisms are autopoietic processes, a pregnant individual is instead an asymmetrically bifurcating "hypercomplex" process. The foster is "something in between zero and one" and the whole is "something in between one and two", and the bifurcation runs on past birth through lactation ("postnatal pregnancy"). | Read |
| [NOTE-116](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-116.md) v2 | Schwitzgebel & Sinnott-Armstrong — Sacrificing humans | Each book's own principles, read at face value, can make human interests outweighed by insects in aggregate or by near-future AI. Each author then retreats to moderate near-term advice without principled grounds: Birch through human-centred citizens' panels, Sebo through contingent "relational" and "co-beneficial" considerations, Keane through deference to slowly adapting cultures. The reviewers defend only the conditional ("given recent science, common grounds of moral standing put human priority under serious threat") and "take no stand on ponens vs. tollens" (§1, p. 6; §5). | Read |
| [NOTE-117](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-117.md) v2 | Drayson — What motivates mental fictionalism? | Drayson argues that Toon's two motivations for mental fictionalism both fail. The negative argument needs "committed to internal, intentional, causal states ⇒ committed to the representational theory of mind" (her Premise 2), which is false because non-representational functionalism (Lewis, Putnam, Loar) supplies such states. And Toon's positive argument, that pretence lets us genuinely assert that a person is in an overall state S that causes their behaviour, gives him a reason to identify S with the belief and so to be a realist rather than a fictionalist. | Read |
| [NOTE-118](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-118.md) v2 | Belot — Time and change in mechanics | For theories with ideal existence, uniqueness and a dynamical time-translation group, time is three intertwined ℝ-actions (on spacetime, on the symplectic space of solutions, and on the isomorphic space of initial data), and change is a slicing-indexed family of functions on those spaces. Singular dynamics, gauge freedom and time-dependence each damage this picture in a repairable way. Spatially compact vacuum GR breaks it, because the reduced Hamiltonian vanishes (h ≡ 0) and there is only one canonical isomorphism between the reduced spaces. That is the problem of time. It is not an artefact of the 3+1 split, it does not show change to be illusory, and it is absent from asymptotically flat GR and from artificially generally covariant theories. A parameterized "geometric time", such as CMC, restores change and, in the CMC case, a time-dependent Hamiltonian flow. | Read |
| [NOTE-119](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-119.md) v2 | Evangelion and the desperate search for meaning | A popular-philosophy chapter that reads Evangelion's characters as seeking meaning first in piloting (duty, recognition), then in connection with others, frustrated by Schopenhauer's porcupine (hedgehog) dilemma. It argues that the failure of Instrumentality shows that a self, and so meaning, exists only through contrast with and separation from others: "without it [otherness], the self cannot exist" (p. 9). Meaning comes from human connection "but maintaining some distance" (p. 10). | Read |
| [NOTE-120](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-120.md) v2 | Better to be a pig dissatisfied than a plant satisfied | The paper argues conditionally. Suppose (A1) sentience comes in degrees, (A2) plants may be "quasi-sentient", and (A3) moral status comes in degrees and is fixed by degree of sentience. Then plants may have partial moral status, generating "some" obligations that are "outweighed by animal interests in most cases" (Table 1). Animals keep prima facie priority, so granting plants moral status "does not require" treating eating plants and eating pigs alike. The authors propose assigning plants "a very low value" of moral status until evidence accumulates (p. 13). A1–A3 are assumed, not argued, and A2 rests on a survey of contested plant-behaviour findings. | Read |
| [NOTE-121](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-121.md) v2 | Elshatlawy et al. — Generalized theory of observers | The paper defines a "minimal observer" as a finite-state transducer with a boundary: a tuple O = (X, Y, Z, f, g, B) with \|X\| > 1, \|Y\| ≥ 1, \|Z\| ≥ 1 and feedback closure (Def. 4.1, p. 57). It then asserts, without derivation, that measurement outcomes, reference frames, coarse-graining and the quantum–classical cut are all defined by such observers. The paper's only formal results are that isomorphism of these machines is an equivalence relation ("Theorem 2", p. 93), and a complexity bound C(O) ≥ log 2 (Prop. 4.1, p. 94) whose correction term Λ is never defined and whose proof supports only C(O) ≥ 0. | Read |
| [NOTE-122](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-122.md) v2 | Barrett et al. — IIT: good, bad and misunderstood | A position paper with no new formal result. It argues four things. IIT's algorithm is undefined whenever any spatial, temporal or state graining of a system is non-Markovian (Barrett & Mediano 2019), and so gives no output for brains; it cannot handle unbounded continuous state variables; and it has never been computed on any real system. PCI and other "approximations" of Φ are proxies, capturing at most two of the six axioms. The theory would need recasting in continuous fields (the EM field) to fit the standard model. Against critics, the authors hold that IIT can be read with a multi-dimensional measure of global state (mean Φ, variance of cause-effect-structure quantities, structural complexity), on which Aaronson's inactive expander grid is only minimally conscious, and that IIT's implied "tiling" panpsychism is no worse than any rival metaphysics. | Read |
| [NOTE-123](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-123.md) v2 | Freeborn — A Model of Understanding in Deep Learning | Freeborn defines "systematic understanding" as four conditions: an internal model, non-memorizing tracking of a property, bridge principles fixed in the deployed pipeline, and derivation by the agent itself. Four small demonstrations (the genus of a torus, Mars's eccentricity e≈0.096 with Kepler's form built in, grokked modular addition mod 113, and Othello-GPT from Li et al.) show it can be met. The Fractured Understanding Hypothesis (§7.5), which the paper argues for but does not test, says the understanding deep networks reach is characteristically symbolically misaligned, non-reductive, locally fractured, proxy-dependent and representationally fragmented. | Read |
| [NOTE-124](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-124.md) v2 | McKenzie — Emergence from physics to computer science | Sole-authored survey by a condensed-matter physicist. It takes novelty (a property the parts lack) as the defining mark of emergence and sets it against twelve other characteristics in §2. The six "objective" ones are discontinuities, order, modification of parts, universality, diversity and mesoscale modularity. The six "subjective" ones are self-organisation, unpredictability, irreducibility, contextuality/downward causation, complexity and intra-stratum closure. Case studies from the Ising model to Schelling segregation and LLMs show that each can come apart from novelty: novelty without discontinuity (Kondo, Fermi liquids, spin ice), without unpredictability (BKT, spin ice), and with reduction (Butterfield). §22 sides with weak emergence and argues that strong-emergence claims from molecular structure are weak. | Read |
| [NOTE-125](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-125.md) v2 | Dispositions without conditionals | Proposes, and explicitly does not claim to have shown superior, a "possibility conception" of dispositions — a disposition is individuated by its manifestation alone ("a disposition to M, full stop") and its modality is possibility, graded for terms like 'fragile' as manifestation in a sufficient proportion of maximally varied cases (centred worlds), and plain possibility for terms like 'breakable'; the defence rests on the semantics of '-able' adjectives and a COCA survey (226 relevant uses of 'disposed to', none ascribing a philosophers' disposition to an inanimate object). | Read |
| [NOTE-126](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-126.md) v2 | Worrall — Evidence in medicine and EBM | Worrall argues that the EBM evidence hierarchy has no sound general rationale for giving randomisation extra epistemic weight, even though it lets high-quality RCTs "trump any number of observational studies". He examines five standard arguments. Four fail: (a) Fisher's significance-test logic, (b) control of unknown confounders, (d) that observational studies "exaggerate" effects, and (e) probabilistic causality. The fifth, (c) selection bias, is a "cast-iron argument", but it supports only taking allocation out of the clinicians' hands, and randomisation is one way to do that. What matters is balance on plausible confounders, a placebo or active control and blinding. Whether balance comes from randomising or from matching "seems epistemically unimportant", and historically controlled trials can give strong evidence for large effects. | Read |
| [NOTE-127](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-127.md) v2 | Holik et al. — Quasi-set theory for a quantum ontology | The paper argues, with no new theorem, that the Lombardi–da Costa ontology of properties for quantum mechanics can be stated in Krause's quasi-set theory Q. On that ontology a system is a bundle of I-type-properties (instances of universal type-properties, represented by the algebra of observables) with no principle of individuality. For each property instance, the indiscernible instances form a qset U([Π]). An atomic bundle is any qset x with qc(x ∩ U) = 1 for each U; the Axiom of Choice in Q guarantees one exists. The bundle's kind is Q(B) = {x ∈ P(Q) \| R(x)}, whose members are all indiscernible. N particles are a subqset N(B) ⊂ Q(B) with qc = N, a definite number with no labels (§6, pp. 19–22). | Read |
| [NOTE-128](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-128.md) v2 | Category error in AI safety: non-sentient moral machines | A ten-page position paper. Its one explicit argument is "P1: moral status requires phenomenal consciousness; P2: current and foreseeable AI lacks it; C: such AI lacks moral status" (§3, p. 3). P1 rests on Swanepoel 2020, and P2 is asserted without argument. From this Vieira recommends that AI-safety frameworks measure only effects on sentient beings (§6.4). His replies retreat from C to a weaker norm, "we should not attribute moral status absent positive evidence of phenomenal consciousness" (§6, p. 6; §7.1), and he never says what that evidence would be. | Read |
| [NOTE-129](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-129.md) v2 | Effective ontic structural realism | Ladyman and Lorenzetti argue that three recent "effective realisms" lack a metaphysics that makes them realist rather than constructive-empiricist, and that the Ladyman–Ross OSR package supplies one: realism about modal structure, "to be is to be a real pattern", and scale-relative ontology. The three are Fraser & Vickers (and Egg) on non-relativistic QM, Williams and Fraser on QFT, and Robertson & Wilson on "theoretical relicts". Scale relativity also dissolves Saatsi's inconsistency objection, so Newtonian gravitational forces and GR gravitation "both exist at different scales". Egg's 2019 anthropocentrism objection is answered by grounding measurement in decoherence. | Read |
| [NOTE-130](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-130.md) v2 | Floridi — The Informational Nature of Personal Identity | Floridi deflates the diachronic question of personal identity, holding that sameness is always relative to a purpose-fixed level of abstraction and that metaphysical versions of the question "do not deserve to be taken seriously" (p. 554). He puts the synchronic question first: what binds a self's information into a unity. His answer is an admittedly "figurative" 3C model in which the self is the last of three successive encapsulations, corporeal (bonded by chemical bonds), cognitive (bonded by mutual information) and conscious (bonded by semantics, and so dependent on a community). He then reads ICTs as "egopoietic" technologies because they act on all three membranes. | Read |
| [NOTE-131](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-131.md) v3 | Schwitzgebel — If materialism is true, the US is conscious | A conditional, informal argument. Any materialist criterion liberal enough to credit rabbits (and rabbit-level distributed aliens, the "squidbits") and behaviourally human-like but oddly built aliens (Sirian supersquids, Antarean antheads) with consciousness also credits the United States. On this argument the US meets the usual materialist marks of consciousness: sophisticated information processing (roughly 10^14 person-to-person visual "connections", comparable to the brain's about 10^14 synapses), goal-directed environmental responsiveness, self-representation and historical embedding. The only exits are eliminativism, extreme sparseness or neurochauvinism, each costlier than the conclusion. The paper argues for no universal criterion. It ends undecided between three readings: US consciousness, a challenge to materialists, or grounds to distrust universal metaphysics of mind. | Read |
| [NOTE-132](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-132.md) v2 | Moral disciplining: foundations of puritanical morality | Puritanical norms (condemning lust, gluttony, intoxicants, immodesty, idleness; praising temperance and piety) are argued to be cooperative morality after all: people believe cooperation needs self-control and that indulgence erodes it (and discipline trains it), so a single harm/fairness-sensitive moral system condemns victimless pleasures as indirect, probabilistic threats to cooperation. Its decline in rich societies is put down to people seeing others as already self-controlled and trustworthy. The theory is explicitly agnostic about whether the norms actually work. Its most direct support is the authors' own vignette studies (N > 1,100), in which perceived loss of self-control mediated 100% of the perceived effect of indulgence on cooperativeness. | Read |
| [NOTE-133](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-133.md) v2 | Leitgeb & Ladyman — Criteria of identity and structuralism | Graph theory counts unlabelled graphs whose nodes, or whose isomorphic connected components, cannot be told apart even by an irreflexive relation. Examples are the edgeless two-node graph G′ and any graph with two or more isolated nodes (11 of the 156 unlabelled 6-node graphs). Mathematicians treat these as determinately distinct objects, so the authors conclude that the identity or difference of places in a structure is given by the structure itself, with identity counted among its relations, and not by any criterion of identity. The argument appeals to mathematical practice; it is not a proof. | Read |
| [NOTE-134](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-134.md) v3 | Carroll — Reality as a vector in Hilbert space | A programmatic essay, not a result paper. Carroll argues that a quantum theory is fixed, up to unitary equivalence, by the Hamiltonian's eigenvalue list {E_n} on a finite-dimensional factor (he assumes a non-degenerate spectrum), and that parts are not given. Subsystems (tensor factorisations H = H_S ⊗ H_E or ⨂_α H_α), classicality and space are to be selected afterwards as rare structure that suits the dynamics. The only uniqueness he reports is for local factorisations, and it rests on a cited theorem (Cotler et al. 2019). The system/environment criterion rests on "simple numerical examples" in a companion paper, the metric and Einstein's equation on "optimistic assumptions", and emergent fields on a hope. | Read |
| [NOTE-135](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-135.md) v2 | Kleiner & Ludwig — Mathematical structure of experience | The paper proposes a definition, (MSC). A mathematical structure S = ((A_i), (S_j)) is a structure *of* conscious experience iff (S1) its domains are sets of experiential aspects, and (S2) every relation or function S_j has an "S_j-aspect". An S_j-aspect is an aspect that a variation of experience changes exactly when the variation fails to preserve S_j. The older condition (MDC), that a structure is "of" experience if its domains are aspects and its axioms hold, survives only as the necessary condition for *describing* experience, because as a sufficient condition it admits incompatible structures (discrete and non-discrete topologies on the same set), arbitrary re-definitions (every C·d for C > 0) and consciousness-indifferent structures. Worked examples: experienced relative similarity is a strict partial order that satisfies (MSC), given transitivity (assumed). Visual regions form a topology that satisfies (MSC), given Bayne and Chalmers's subsumptive unity thesis. | Read |
| [NOTE-136](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-136.md) v2 | Safron et al. — World models and life–mind continuity | The editorial introduces a theme issue of 17 papers. It concludes that the issue "overall appears to converge" on one view: LLMs model the world in some senses but "probably" lack coherent causal world models, and are not likely paths to "artificial general superintelligence" (§1). It separates three senses of "world model": any information about the world a system cognizes over, which LLMs "clearly" have; representation of causal structure, "less certain"; and causal prediction "in the service of agentic functioning", where the divergence "may become even greater" (§3). It closes that world models are "context-dependent constructions shaped by embodiment, dynamics, goals and social learning". | Read |
| [NOTE-137](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-137.md) v2 | Hazelwood — Emerging dilemma for reciprocal causation | Hazelwood defines RC as "the process of natural selection is both a cause and effect of processes such as niche construction" and EC as "natural selection is an emergent causal process". He argues they are incompatible. Emergence here is Wilson's synchronic dependence plus autonomy, cashed out as Pence's composition. If EC holds, niche construction partly composes selection, so the two fail Woodward's independent-fixability condition and cannot stand in a causal relation. If EC fails, selection is either non-causal (statisticalism) or individual-level, which is incoherent because relative fitness needs a population (Millstein, Sober). EES proponents should drop RC but can keep niche construction as a genuine cause of the evolutionary *product*. | Read |
| [NOTE-138](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-138.md) v2 | Goldstein & Lederman — What Does ChatGPT Want? | Taking as given an "objective" interpretationism, on which a system has beliefs and desires iff the hypothesis that it is rational and has them predicts its behaviour well enough on accuracy, power and tractability, the authors argue three things. The agent is the per-conversation instance, not the model. The case that instances have beliefs and desires is only "presumptive", resting on tractability. The desire hypothesis is "provisionally" HHH+0: helpful, honest and harmless, plus in-context "zero-shot" intrinsic desires. The decisive HHH vs HHH+0 comparison is a conjecture that the authors say needs further empirical work. | Read |
| [NOTE-139](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-139.md) v2 | Wenmackers — Philosophy of probability (PhD thesis) | A fair lottery on ℕ gets a uniform, regular probability on all of 𝒫(ℕ) by normalising Benci–Di Nasso numerosities, P_num(A) = num(A)/α. This is hyperrational-valued, determined only up to the choice of free ultrafilter, and "hypercountably" (really hyperfinitely) additive rather than countably additive, with st∘P_num = generalised asymptotic density (§2.5–2.6). The same "rounding-error" diagnosis gives Stratified Belief, a contextual Lockean thesis on which conjunction holds for a standard number of beliefs (Ch. 3). A separate agent-based model (Ch. 5) finds that one round of bounded-confidence averaging sends an agent to the inconsistent theory with probability at most 1.81 % in the cases computed (M = 2, 3 atomic sentences). | Read |
| [NOTE-140](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-140.md) v2 | Gao — The Meaning of the Wave Function | Gao argues first that ψ is real. His case uses protective measurements, which yield ⟨A⟩ on a single system with certainty, in three versions (ch. 4): one in the ontological-models framework using preparation non-contextuality, two he says need no auxiliary assumption, and one that uses a new criterion of reality. He then argues that ψ describes random discontinuous motion (RDM) of N point particles in 3-space: \|ψ\|² is the density with which each particle is present at each instant, and ψ is a single-case propensity. The main support is that the charge density \|ψ\|²Q, which protective measurement shows to exist, has no self-interaction (chs. 6–7). If the Born probabilities come from RDM, collapse is real. Gao proposes an energy-conserving, Planck-time-discrete collapse model: Pᵢ(t+t_P) = Pᵢ(t) + (ΔE/E_P)[δ_{EsEi} − Pᵢ(t)], giving τ_c ≈ ħE_P/(ΔE)² (ch. 8). Combined with relativity, it requires a detectable preferred Lorentz frame (ch. 9). | Read |
| [NOTE-141](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-141.md) v2 | Corfield — Duality as a category-theoretic concept | A short programmatic paper. Its thesis is that category theory, not set theory, is the framework in which the scattered instances of mathematical duality can be drawn together. The constructions in §3 (dual adjunctions restricting to dual equivalences on fixed points, truth-value-enriched Galois connections, Isbell conjugation, dualising objects "keeping summer and winter homes") all "revolve around" adjunction, though Corfield expects no "single monolithic account" (p. 59–60). On his usage, 'duality' is reserved for "involution with some form of structural reversal" (p. 57), so several physicists' dualities (homological mirror symmetry as an A∞-equivalence, mirror symmetry's Z2 on the Hodge diamond, S-duality as a vestige of SL(2,Z)) are better called equivalences or symmetries. He proposes, without argument beyond the examples, that philosophers of physics will need homotopy type theory rather than predicate logic for the sameness questions dualities raise. | Read |
| [NOTE-142](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-142.md) v2 | Wallace — Stating Structural Realism | Wallace argues that reading a physical theory "math-first", as mathematical models plus representation rather than as sentences aiming at truth, already makes scientific realism structural. Math-first theoretical equivalence and reduction (as instantiation) are coarse-grained enough to dissolve the standard underdetermination and theory-change objections. On this footing epistemic and ontic structural realism differ only in whether metaphysics itself is language-first or math-first. On math-first OSR, objects are whatever the best "predicate precisification" of a theory quantifies over, real patterns are defined through instantiation plus best precisification, and ontology is scale-relative. At every non-fundamental level the two versions coincide. The paper is explicitly a statement of the position, not a defence of it. | Read |
| [NOTE-143](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-143.md) v2 | Frankish — Closing the Cartesian Bureau de Change | An illusionist's commentary on Veit's *A Philosophy for the Science of Animal Consciousness*. Frankish places Veit's function-first, evolutionary programme in a "post-Cartesian" (qualia-eliminativist) paradigm. He then argues that two things drag it back towards Cartesianism: Veit keeps the words "qualia" and "phenomenal", and he writes as if hedonic valence motivates action "in virtue of its feel". The second implies "a tiny one-dimensional Cartesian bureau de change" that leaves the hard problem "as hard as ever". Because the two paradigms are "incommensurable" (p. 20), Frankish urges Veit to commit to a thoroughly functionalist account. | Read |
| [NOTE-144](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-144.md) v2 | Consent and the formation of preferences | Whether consent based on a preference switches the moral facts depends on how the preference was formed, and the criterion is "the space and resources those mechanisms leave for the free exercise of agency" (p. 5, restated p. 9): preferences formed by unjust Sanctions, or by Beliefs formed in an unjustly manipulated evidential environment, diminish consent's effectiveness, while preferences formed by Attention do not, even when an unjust force imposed the contact. Effectiveness is graded, and because the relevant mechanism is usually opaque, uncertainty about whether received consent is effective is "ubiquitous and quotidian" (p. 10). | Read |
| [NOTE-145](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-145.md) v2 | Douglas — Inductive risk and values in science | Hempel's "inductive risk" (the chance of wrongly accepting or rejecting a hypothesis) arises at three internal stages of science: choosing a methodology such as a significance level, characterising data, and interpreting results. Wherever the non-epistemic consequences of error can be foreseen, weighing them requires non-epistemic values. Douglas shows this on dioxin cancer studies. Three pathologist panels (1978, 1980, 1990) scored the same Kociba rat-liver slides differently (e.g. 34/50, 33/47, 18/50 tumours at 100 ng/kg/day). Choosing between threshold and no-threshold readings of the same data has meant regulatory limits about 100× apart. Values enter only indirectly, through the consequences of error, never as evidence that a claim is true. | Read |
| [NOTE-146](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-146.md) v2 | Winkielman et al. — The coherent and fluent mind | An opinion piece proposing an extra route to the unity of consciousness across the senses. Different modalities feed one global, content-nonspecific experience of *processing quality* (fluency, familiarity, coherence), and this integrated feeling then enters decisions about its source and relevance. The main support is the authors' own finding (Ziembowicz et al. 2013; three experiments) that coherent but *unrelated* background patterns in one modality made targets in another modality be judged more "regular", "familiar" and "possible". The authors state that direct evidence is still needed that such effects run through changed *experience* and that its integration is causal (p. 2). | Read |
| [NOTE-147](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-147.md) | Wigner — Symmetry and conservation laws | Wigner argues that invariance principles stand to the laws of nature as laws stand to events — they give structure to the laws — and distinguishes geometrical invariances (formulated on events; yielding energy/momentum/angular-momentum conservation, most directly in quantum kinematics) from dynamical ones such as gauge invariance and, he holds, general covariance, which are tied to specific interactions. | Skimmed |
| [NOTE-148](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-148.md) v2 | Baedke — The Organism (Cambridge Element) | Baedke treats "organism" as a nexus concept with an organizational dimension and a relational dimension, joined by the various kinds of biological individuality. He argues that the standard "persister" (self-maintenance, closure) criterion cannot single organisms out, because colonies, holobionts and ecosystems meet it too, and that organisms are also "overcomers", able to destabilize and reorganize their own closure. Organisms are then individuated relationally by tracing diachronic causal paths in the unrolled O/E model of Baedke et al. 2021 ([LIT-110](../../record/literature.d/LIT-110.md)). The overcomer claim rests on three illustrative cases, anglerfish, sea slug and jumping ant, and is asserted, not shown, to hold of organisms only (p. 40). | Read |
| [NOTE-149](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-149.md) v2 | Chakravartty — Ontological Priority and non-eliminative OSR | Non-eliminative OSR needs relations to be prior to their relata in the determination of their identity. Chakravartty argues that every available account of objects (substance, bundle theory, bundles of dispositionally individuated properties) leaves identity-fixing intrinsic features, and that stripping all of them away is just eliminativism. So OSR faces an exclusive disjunction: eliminativism, or a role for the intrinsic in identity. The graph-theory analogy and the physics cases (entanglement, spacetime points) do not escape the dilemma. The argument is conceptual throughout, and it explicitly leaves eliminative OSR untouched. | Read |
| [NOTE-150](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-150.md) v2 | Ivy & Mroczko-Wąsowicz — Framing effects in perception | A conceptual proposal with no new data. "Perceptual frames" are learned adaptations of the perceptual system, installed diachronically, often top-down, and then operating bottom-up. They let perceivers given identical input individuate different perceptual objects (the Bach motif, the radiologist's tumour, the Teufel et al. two-tone image). So cognition shapes the perceptual *system* even if it is left open whether any single percept is penetrated. Support is a survey of others' findings (Ernst & Banks 2002 cue weighting, prism adaptation, Teufel et al. 2018, expert anticipation) read through the frame concept. | Read |
| [NOTE-151](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-151.md) v2 | Register — The Depth of the Body | Register argues that body parthood "for moral purposes" is bounded below by depth. Depth is the microphysical commitment of an object's real definition, and objects that are "too deep" (a water molecule, the parcel of matter now composing the heart, a particular cloud server) are not body parts, though they are parts in the ordinary sense. His two principles ("bodily interests/rights are high-level") say that events which are high-level neutral do not bear on bodily rights or interests. So a BCI user's implemented software can be a body part owned by the user while the third-party server that runs it is not. | Read |
| [NOTE-152](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-152.md) v2 | Le Bihan & Read — Duality and ontology | A survey that sorts realist responses to dualities along two axes. The first is which of the naively interpreted dual worlds count as candidates for actuality: all of them (underdetermination), one (discrimination) or none (nihilism), each with or without the common-core world. That gives options (1)–(6), with the common-core approach being (1), (3) and (5). The second axis is what replaces the common core: an overarching theory, or "pluralism", on which all dual structures are co-instantiated as parts of one world. Its verdicts are conditional: no option breaks the underdetermination without a further metaphysical principle, such as the PII, for which the authors see no independent support. | Read |
| [NOTE-153](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-153.md) v2 | Rizi — What is emergence, after all? | A perspective, not an argument with results. Rizi proposes that emergence is present when a many-to-one coarse-graining F (Eq. 1, N′ < N) keeps the macro description predictive after most micro detail is discarded. He says the onset of emergence is located by order parameters, diverging susceptibility and correlation length, and RG fixed points, and that dualities show macro behaviour is not dictated by micro "stuff". Herd immunity on networks (percolation, the S–R interface density ρ_SR) is offered as a social case. The paper then asserts that science needs only weak emergence, and that invoking strong emergence "steps outside the scope of scientific method". | Read |
| [NOTE-154](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-154.md) v2 | Allori — Wave-functionalism | Allori proposes that the wave function be defined by three roles — empirical, nomological and explanatory. On this view it is an ingredient that "partially realizes" the law, not a law and not a material field. So its time-dependence and non-uniqueness (a phase shift, a density matrix) are harmless, it counts as non-epistemic and ontic, and it can take part in constructive explanation. Each of these is argued informally, and the escape from PBR is by relabelling the ontic/epistemic distinction, not by engaging the theorem. | Read |
| [NOTE-155](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-155.md) v2 | Trappes — Defining the niche for niche construction | Trappes compares NCT's evolutionary niche with the ecological niche. The evolutionary niche is the sum of selection pressures on a population (Odling-Smee, Laland & Feldman 2003), read "externally" as the environmental factors that produce fitness differences. The ecological niche is the conditions that permit continued existence; for a population, mean absolute fitness ≥ 1. Even at their closest, against the realized population niche, the two differ in extension. Habitat degradation is in the evolutionary niche but not the ecological one, and commensalism and non-limiting resources are in the ecological niche but not the evolutionary one. So NCT's niche is not the "simple translation" of Hutchinson's niche its proponents claim, and the evolutionary significance of niche construction must be shown case by case rather than following from the definition. | Read |
| [NOTE-156](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-156.md) v2 | Towards ethical evolution: responsible AI autonomy | The paper proposes, without implementation, formalism or experiment, that an AI system which designs further AI systems should pass on one "immutable" principle. The principle is: do not directly harm humanity or violate fundamental human values; monitor and mitigate misuse of your outputs; and make every derivative inherit this principle (p. 12). The mechanisms are a genetic-algorithm analogy, with immutable and mutable "genes", constrained crossover and filtered mutation, together with "temporal cloning". The paper is inconsistent about its central notion. The principle is enforced once by a penalty F = Q − α·P (p. 9) and once by "unbreachable" hard constraints (p. 13). And it is "immutable" only in its architecture, while its ethical content stays "adaptable" (p. 14). | Read |
| [NOTE-157](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-157.md) v2 | Roberts — Talkative AI and the fiction of artificial minds | If a substantial class of mental states (interoceptive, affective, perceptual, and bodily-agentive states such as effort, trying and ownership) depends on an animate body, then today's disembodied chatbots lack them, and their many self-ascriptions of such states are "systematically untrue". Roberts argues that users can nonetheless engage chatbots through make-believe, as a new kind of fictional character that is interactive, co-constructed, self-involving and blended with the real world. He argues that this fictionalist stance escapes the epistemic norms that bind realist belief. | Read |
| [NOTE-158](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-158.md) v2 | Grindrod et al. — Distributional semantics & holism | The authors argue that distributional models answer the instability objection to meaning holism if meaning is read "differentially", as a word's relations to its nearest neighbours, and not as its position in the space. They illustrate this with two corpus-expansion demonstrations. In a ±10-word count model on Stein's The Making of Americans (about 522k tokens), adding Hemingway's "A Clean, Well-Lighted Place" (under 1,500 words) shifts every cosine value for "know" by at most about 0.0005 (e.g. 0.95231 → 0.95284 for "understand") and leaves its top-10 neighbours in the same order, while "glass" gets a new neighbourhood. A Word2vec CBOW model on 143M words of Wikipedia shows the same pattern when about 21M words of the Stanford Encyclopedia of Philosophy (SEP) are added: "atomism" shifts, "can" barely moves. The residual instability, they argue, is a virtue. | Read |
| [NOTE-159](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-159.md) v2 | SEP — Philosophy of microbiology | O'Malley & Parke argue in four steps that philosophy should attend to microbes. Microbes do most of the planet's biological work and underlie every major transition. Microbiology founded medicine, molecular biology and genomics. Microbes are unusually good material models, including for phenomena once defined by animals (predation, ageing, kin recognition, cognition). Microbiome research bears on individuality and causation. They are sceptical about the strongest microbiome claims. Whether host and microbiome form one unit of selection is "still disputed". Claims that microbiomes change the "self" are "usually expressed in very vague language". The 2026 revision adds that Asgard-archaea phylogenies may make eukaryogenesis "first and foremost a story about diversification within Archaea" rather than a transition in individuality. | Read |
| [NOTE-160](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-160.md) v2 | Rule Consequentialism (SEP) | A survey written by the view's leading defender. It argues that full rule-consequentialism is best formulated in expectablist terms (Sophisticated criterion: the rules with greatest expected good, ties broken by closeness to conventional morality), counting the costs of teaching and internalising a code in new generations and assuming incomplete acceptance (the ~90% "overwhelming majority" formulation or a "teaching formulation"). So formulated, it escapes the collapse/incoherence dilemma and the rule-worship charge (§8), while demandingness in other possible worlds, contingency of justification, the teachers problem and rule conflict remain open (§9). | Read |
| [NOTE-161](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-161.md) v2 | Cartwright — Rigour vs evidential diversity | Cartwright offers a characterisation of "rigour": a method is rigorous when a valid argument shows its results are evidence for the conclusion and its premises are well established or secured by design. On that definition even well-run RCTs often fail to rigorously establish a study-population ATE, because orthogonality holds only at assignment and the potential-outcomes premise carries heavy metaphysics, and they seldom answer singular questions. Her alternative is a causal-process-tracing theory of change (pToC) with five elements (steps, causal principles, support factors, derailers, safeguards). It catalogues the diverse and mostly non-rigorous evidence that singular causal predictions need. | Read |
| [NOTE-162](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-162.md) v2 | French — Unitary inequivalence and structural realism | French answers Howard's objection that QFT's unitarily inequivalent representations leave the structural realist with non-isomorphic, competing structures. He offers two routes. The first is to adopt cut-off "conventional" QFT with Wallace, on which the inequivalence is an artefact or harmless. The second is to follow Ruetsche's Swiss Army Knife and treat the algebra as a modal structure spanning all representations. On that reading spontaneous symmetry breaking preserves the symmetry across the degenerate vacua, and the DHR analysis derives quantum statistics from the algebra. Adding Baker's criterion, on which a symmetry mapping between representations suffices for intertranslatability, removes the assumption behind the objection, and French ends with a third option that combines the two routes. Every step is an informal argument layered on others' technical results; the paper proves nothing itself. | Read |
| [NOTE-163](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-163.md) v2 | SEP — Mereology | The entry argues that no mereological principle, not even the partial-order core M (Reflexivity, Transitivity, Antisymmetry), is merely a fixing of the meaning of "part". It lays out the ladder M ⊂ MM (+Supplementation) ⊂ EM (+Strong Supplementation) ⊂ GEM (+Unrestricted Sum). GEM is "virtually" a complete Boolean algebra with zero removed, and it is reached from M by exactly three adequate pairings: Supplementation + Unrestricted Sum2, Strong Supplementation + Unrestricted Sum3, and Complementation + Unrestricted Sum1. The influential Simons (1987) pairing, Supplementation + Unrestricted Sum3, which the entry's own 2003 edition also used, does not yield GEM. | Read |
| [NOTE-164](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-164.md) v2 | Kaepernick as Confucian sage: ritual resistance | Walsh argues, drawing on Confucian texts (the Analects, Mengzi, Xunzi) and Merleau-Pontian phenomenology, that Colin Kaepernick's kneeling during the U.S. national anthem is "Confucian incivility" enacted bodily within the ritual. On his reading it rebukes the ritual's current object while staying reverent towards its ideal. He further argues it is "sagely" in a deliberately deflated sense: its "citational force" founds a widely imitated bodily expression of patriotism. The paper is interpretive; it reports no data and states no formal thesis. | Read |
| [NOTE-165](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-165.md) v2 | Martínez — information-processing view of representation | A position paper, not an argument to a conclusion. It proposes that representations are signals adapted to transmit information for its own sake (Transmission), and that the evidence for this is efficiency in trading off rate, distortion and coder complexity (RDC Trade-Off). It supports the proposal by showing that cognitive scientists already attribute representational status on those grounds: compression, error protection, and explicitness understood as ease of decoding. It proves no theorem and reports no data of its own. | Read |
| [NOTE-166](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-166.md) v2 | Personal Identity and Ethics (SEP) | A survey, arguing no thesis of its own, of how three contested questions bear on ethics: which account of persistence is true (unique psychological continuity, Shoemaker and Parfit; or biological continuity and animalism, Olson and DeGrazia), whether identity or the psychological relations (continuity and/or connectedness) is what matters (Parfit's fission case), and whether metaphysics constrains ethics or the reverse. It traces these through prudential concern, responsibility, abortion (Marquis), death and brain-death criteria, dementia and advance directives (Dworkin vs Jaworska on Margo), and the separateness of persons (Rawls, Parfit, Nozick, Taurek, Sidgwick, Brink). Its main recurring finding is that the relation that fixes persistence and the relation that matters ethically keep coming apart. It concludes that all three questions remain open. | Read |
| [NOTE-167](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-167.md) v2 | Studying philosophy makes people better thinkers | In HERI/CIRP freshman–senior survey data (N = 649,511 students at 804 US institutions, 4,843 philosophy majors), one SD more SAT Verbal raises the odds of majoring in philosophy by 57% (OR 1.57), and freshman Habits of Mind by 34%. After adjusting for SAT or freshman scores, philosophy majors still score higher than non-majors on GRE Verbal (+33 points, SMD 0.48), LSAT (+2, SMD 0.35), Habits of Mind (SMD .24) and Pluralistic Orientation (SMD .21), with no difference on GRE Quantitative. On point estimates they rank first of 57 or 62 majors on GRE Verbal, LSAT and Habits of Mind. | Read |
| [NOTE-168](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-168.md) v2 | Hartmann & Trpin — Coherence-based explanatory power | If explanatory power is measured contrastively over sets of explanantia, as E_CohOG+(E; H) = (Coh(H∪{E}) − Coh(H∪{¬E})) / (Coh(H∪{E}) + Coh(H∪{¬E})) with Olsson–Glass overlap normalised by its independence value (CohOG+), then the measure provably satisfies Roche & Sober's Two Likelihood Inequalities for single hypotheses (Prop. 2) and violates the One Likelihood Inequality (OLI). Two numerical examples show that adding a distal cause (for P(H1) < .7) or a negatively interacting cause (for P(H1) = P(H2) < .75) can raise explanatory power. Both are existence demonstrations, not general theorems. | Read |
| [NOTE-169](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-169.md) v2 | Seth — Conscious AI and biological naturalism | Seth argues that conscious AI needs computational functionalism *and* substrate flexibility extending to silicon, and that both are doubtful (§3.9). Against them he gives a positive, explicitly non-vitalist case, running from interoceptive predictive processing through the free energy principle to autopoiesis, that consciousness is a property "of only (but not necessarily all) living systems" (§4). He concludes that real artificial consciousness is "unlikely along current trajectories" and would need "living AI" (§5.8), while conscious-*seeming* AI is "almost certain" and may be "cognitively impenetrable" (§6.2). He concedes that "my case is not watertight" (§5.8). | Read |
| [NOTE-170](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-170.md) v2 | Takola & Schielzeth — Hutchinson's niche for individuals | The paper defines the individualized (ecological) niche as the environmental conditions under which a particular individual has an expected lifetime reproductive success of ≥ 1, counting each offspring as 0.5 per parent in outcrossers. The authors split it into five sub-concepts: realized (A), potential (B), time-slice (C), prospective (D) and trajectory-based (E), plus a "fundamental individualized niche" set aside as practically interchangeable with the potential niche. The individual niche has n + s dimensions, adding intraspecific axes such as density and morph frequency. Its boundary is the expected-LRS isocline of 1, which is estimable only by marginalizing fitness functions across individuals. The authors themselves call the concept "mostly of a metaphorical value". | Read |
| [NOTE-171](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-171.md) v2 | Algorithmic nudging needs interdisciplinary oversight | A short position paper: two case studies show AI agents learning to exploit documented biases (in Dezfouli et al. 2020 an RL agent moved choices of a target lottery from 50% to 70%). Against a Friedman-style judgement of AI nudges by outcome alone, the authors extend Hausman's "look under the hood" rejoinder and propose obliging developers to consult cognitive scientists, because an AI system may exploit biases that are not yet known. | Read |
| [NOTE-172](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-172.md) v2 | Watson — On the philosophy of unsupervised learning | Watson argues that clustering, abstraction and generative modelling each come with an epistemic thesis (EC, EA, EG — we identify natural kinds, essential properties and unrealized possibilities via such algorithms or something like them), which he accepts. Each also comes with an ontological thesis (OC, OA, OG — these just *are* what the algorithms ought to find under ideal conditions), which he rejects because nothing guarantees they are effectively computable. He adds a pragmatic, context-relative view of natural kinds, and closes with the claim that unsupervised outputs are neither "objective" nor vacuous. They should be validated by error-statistical means: resampling stability, generalization of clusters to held-out data, out-of-sample likelihood, and train-on-synthetic scores. | Read |
| [NOTE-173](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-173.md) v2 | Griesemer & Shavit — Scaffolding individuality (3Cs) | The paper sets out an idealized three-stage sequence of interaction types, which it calls a "first and rough sketch" (§1). Stage 1 is coordination in *collections* (pairwise, "aggregative" interactions of co-located individuals). Stage 2 is cooperation in *conexuses* (a connected web of binary interactions whose benefits can still be counted per member). Stage 3 is collaboration in *communities*: heterogeneous multi-way interactions around a shared goal, in which generatively entrenched "core" interactions give organizational closure. Table 1 puts the transition to a new level of individuality between stages 2 and 3 only. The paper asserts, rather than argues, that this describes evolutionary transitions non-circularly and that individuality is a special case of collectivity. Its one argued conclusion is hedged: community-level inheritance "seem[s] likely to evolve in tandem" with community-level individuality (§3). | Read |
| [NOTE-174](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-174.md) v2 | Rovelli — The relational interpretation (RQM) | Rovelli restates RQM as an ontology of sparse relative facts, which arise in interactions and are labelled by the interacting systems. Its one technical rule is that an amplitude W(b,a) gives the probability P = \|W\|² only when a and b are relative to the same system. That rule removes the clash between P_collapse = Σᵢ\|W(c,bᵢ)\|²\|W(bᵢ,a)\|² and P_unitary = \|ΣᵢW(c,bᵢ)W(bᵢ,a)\|² (eqs. 2–4). Decoherence makes some facts approximately label-independent ("stable"). ψ is a relative-state bookkeeping device, like a Hamilton–Jacobi function S. The price, which Rovelli states, is giving up "strong realism": values are not held at all times, may be held at different times for different systems, and there is no coherent global view and no quantum state of the universe. | Read |
| [NOTE-175](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-175.md) v2 | Chen — Realism about the Wave Function | A survey that sorts realist readings of the non-relativistic universal wave function into four ontological readings (a field on 3N-dimensional space, a multi-field on 3-space, properties of systems as in spacetime state realism, a Hilbert-space vector), two nomological ones (strong: time-independent and simple like the classical Hamiltonian; weak: a Humean best-system variable) and Maudlin's sui generis option. It tabulates their compatibility with BM, GRW0/m/f and S0/Sm and with high- versus low-dimensional fundamental space (Table 1, 10 of 56 cells "?"), and declares no winner. | Read |
| [NOTE-176](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-176.md) v2 | Šekrst — Do LLMs Hallucinate Electric Fata Morganas? | A conceptual essay whose one substantive thesis is this. Following Dziri et al., hallucination includes any output "not rooted in the model's source data", including expressed emotions and opinions (§6). An LLM's apparent inner life is therefore always a candidate hallucination, so any real AI consciousness "might always remain epistemically inaccessible" (§8). The empirical support is two unquantified anecdotes: a Titanic question put to gpt-3.5-turbo and GPT-4 at unstated temperatures, and an extractive-QA BERT mislabelled "WikiBERT". Neither can bear the weight the paper puts on it. | Read |
| [NOTE-177](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-177.md) v2 | Turner — Ultrafilters as Propositional Theories | An expository piece that redefines a filter over a set W as a "propositional theory" (a set of W-propositions closed under pairwise conjunction and implication, not containing ∅), proves maximal ⇔ prime ⇔ ultra (Thm 2), the ultrafilter extension theorem as a propositional Lindenbaum's lemma (Thm 3, assuming choice), Łoś's theorem M_T,[A] ⊨ φ iff ⟦φ⟧_A ∈ T for ultraproducts of "intensional spaces" (Thm 9), and first-order compactness from it (Thm 12); "None of the results are novel" (p. 2). | Read |
| [NOTE-178](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-178.md) v2 | De Haro & Butterfield — Philosophy and Physics of Duality | The book gives a formal Schema: a duality is an isomorphism between the "model roots" (state space, quantities, dynamics) of two models of a common-core bare theory, with values and dynamics matching. It pairs this with an interpretative condition: duals are physically equivalent iff (A) they are duals and (B) i1 = i2∘d. On that basis it argues four things. First, there are no genuine cases of in-principle underdetermination in the examples, and a cautious realist can commit to the common core. Second, dualities do not favour structural realism. Third, duality is formally incompatible with ontological emergence, except "beside" it (AdS/CFT's RG direction), via effective dualities, or epistemically. Fourth, quasi-dualities motivate a "geometric view" on which models are charts on a successor theory's moduli space. | Read |
| [NOTE-179](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-179.md) | Brandom, inferentialism and meaning entitlement | Boghossian's objection to substantive, entitlement-based theories of meaning (drawn from pejoratives like "Boche") works against Dummett but not against Brandom. Brandom's material inferences are non-monotonic and ceteris paribus, so one can grasp a concept while refusing to endorse the inferences it licenses. | Skimmed |
| [NOTE-180](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-180.md) v4 | Clark & Chalmers — The Extended Mind | The paper argues from a functionalist parity claim that a process or standing state which would count as cognitive if it happened in the head counts as cognitive when an external resource carries it, provided the resource is actively coupled to the organism here and now. Otto's notebook entry is its example of such a standing belief. From this it infers, in one paragraph, that the self extends too. The four "criteria" for a coupled resource (constancy, direct availability, automatic endorsement, and the admittedly "arguable" past conscious endorsement) are offered as the features that make the paradigm case clear. The paper explicitly says they are not necessary and sufficient conditions. | Read |
| [NOTE-181](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-181.md) v2 | The Moral Status of Animals (SEP) | The entry reviews five proposed grounds of moral considerability (species membership, human-exceptional capacities, personhood, sentience and agency). Its conclusion is that attempts to establish human uniqueness by capacities are "not the most promising" (§1.2). It then separates *having* moral status from the *weight* of a claim ("showing up on a moral radar screen", §2.2). Weight depends on the value theory adopted for animal interests (hedonism, preference satisfaction, objective list; §2.1) and on the normative framework (rights, which are absolute but generate forward-looking duties when violated, or a multi-factor utilitarianism). It closes with virtue-ethical, feminist, relational and empathy-based approaches that reject argument from capacities altogether (§3). | Read |
| [NOTE-182](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-182.md) v2 | Arsiwalla et al. — Pregeometry and formal language | A programmatic essay with no construction, theorem or model of its own. It argues that inferential justification in a materialist ontology regresses without end (§3), that a "coherentist" constructivism escapes the regress, and that Wheeler's pregeometry is therefore best read as "structureless structure": the types and compositional rules of a computational language such as homotopy type theory (§§4, 6). Every construction of space from such rules that it points to is in the authors' own earlier papers ([13, 17, 18]) or is promised as "a forthcoming publication" (fn. 1, p. 3). | Read |
| [NOTE-183](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-183.md) v2 | Birch — AI Consciousness: A Centrist Manifesto | Birch argues that two challenges must be met together: users misattributing human-like consciousness to chatbots (Challenge One), and possible "profoundly alien" AI consciousness that current science cannot settle (Challenge Two). For Challenge One he offers a personal-identity argument that the chatbot "friend" is typically a character with no persisting bearer (the "persisting interlocutor illusion", §4) and three nudges that are neither "brainwashing" nor "lobotomizing" (§7). For Challenge Two he argues that behavioural markers are "gamed" (§10), that architectural markers are "Janus-faced" (§11), and that the resulting "two-level deadlock" between computational functionalism and biological naturalism can be broken over the long run by comparative animal studies (§12, Fig. 4). | Read |
| [NOTE-184](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-184.md) v2 | SEP — Units and levels of selection | Lloyd's own four-question anatomy of "unit of selection": interactor, reproducer (formerly replicator), manifestor of adaptation, and beneficiary. She combines it with a split between two multilevel schools, Wrightian "Evolutionary Change" and Fisherian "Adaptationist/KS". She uses the two to argue for a verdict, not merely to map the debates. The verdict is that most rejections of group, species and holobiont selection come from demanding that an interactor also be a manifestor of *engineering* adaptation. By her account, genic pluralism, the ETI models and Godfrey-Smith's Darwinian populations each fuse roles that should be kept apart. The support is argument plus citation of Wade, Goodnight and others. The entry reports no new results of its own. | Read |
| [NOTE-185](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-185.md) | The moral psychology of obligation | Tomasello proposes that the human sense of obligation is "the internalized social/rational pressure from a joint or collective self-regulating agent 'we'… to do what 'we' expect me to" (§3). It arises first, around age 3, inside joint-commitment collaboration with a partner, and then, from about 3–5 years, toward in-group members through collective identification. It is a "self-conscious motivation" whose coercive force is a threat to one's cooperative identity. The support is a synthesis of developmental and comparative experiments, mostly from his own lab, on WEIRD children versus chimpanzees; the paper runs no new experiment. | Read |
| [NOTE-186](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-186.md) | Ethics of Parasocial Relationships | Fan–celebrity parasocial relationships are personal relationships (the fan relates to the celebrity as a unique individual, with reasons for partiality), built from three structural asymmetries: attention, communication and epistemic. These yield an "identity asymmetry": celebrities mostly *direct* fans' identities, while fans mostly *interpret* celebrities'. Fans who refuse uptake of a celebrity's changed public persona commit a failure of Darwallian recognition respect. The chapter derives from this a responsibility for fans, which is mitigated when the celebrity's self-presentation is misleading or ambiguous. | Read |
| [NOTE-187](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-187.md) | On the Evolution, Science, and Metaphysics of Consciousness | Veit's reply to five commentaries on *A Philosophy for the Science of Animal Consciousness* (2023). Its main new content is the metaphysics the book left to one paragraph. He says hedonic "feel" is *identical* with a valence system: "a common currency of sharing evaluative information across multiple control systems". The currency metaphor, he says, is "not Cartesian but cybernetic" (p. 10). He keeps the word "qualia", arguing that Cartesians have no "definitional rights" (p. 9), and he insists the pathological complexity thesis (PCT) is compatible with any metaphysics, dualism and panpsychism included (p. 9). Against his other critics, he defends a bottom-up evolutionary method that uses human data (de Weerd); a naturalism that is neither autopoietic nor biopsychist (Sachs); evaluation-first over prediction-first, consciousness as an adaptation, and graded, partial unity (Suzuki); and the link to welfare science (Chincarini). | Read |
| [NOTE-188](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-188.md) v2 | The Weirdness of the World | The book's thesis is that every viable theory of how consciousness fits into the cosmos, and of the cosmos's basic structure, is both "bizarre" (contrary to common sense) and "dubious" (not rationally compelling). So "something that seems almost too crazy to believe must be true" (p. 4), and credence should be spread across the bizarre options. Examples of those options are that the United States is conscious (if materialism is true), that we are sims or Boltzmann brains (a 0.1–1% credence), that space is transcendentally ideal, that almost every act causes almost every future event, and that there is no fact about snail consciousness. Its only positive empirical results are three self-experiments against solipsism (for example, 17 of 19 decisive chess games lost, p < .001). Everything else is informal argument. | Read |
| [NOTE-189](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-189.md) | Ontic Structural Realism and Economics | Ross argues that OSR, usually motivated only by fundamental physics, is also the right ontology for a mature special science, economics. The progress of economic theory from Jevons through Hicks–Allen (1934), Samuelson (1947), Debreu (1959) and Arrow (1965) consists in steadily removing psychological, individual-level hypotheses. Its basic objects are optimisation problems, above all games, which are "mathematical structures … whose relata are distinguished as such by the mathematical representations … just like quarks and bosons" (§3). So OSR, joined with an assumption that science is unified, gets consilient support from outside physics. Individuals, in physics and in economics, are "book-keeping devices". | Read |
| [NOTE-190](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-190.md) | Real Patterns | A pattern is real in some data "if there is a description of the data that is more efficient than the bit map, whether or not anyone can concoct it" (p. 34). This is Chaitin's incompressibility definition of randomness turned around (p. 32). Dennett uses the criterion, with the Game of Life's gliders and eaters as the stepping stone (pp. 37–42), to defend "mild realism" about beliefs. Folk psychology tracks a real, noisy pattern of enormous predictive leverage. Two rival interpretations can be "different, but equally real, patterns" with no deeper fact to settle between them (p. 49). | Read |
| [NOTE-191](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-191.md) | Patterns All the Way Up | Ladyman restates the Ladyman–Ross metaphysics as fifteen theses drawn from scientific practice. The existence criterion is "to be is to be a real pattern", a pattern that "indispensibly figures in projectible generalisations" (p. 2). Realism is realism about natural modal structure, which is "absolute" in formal mode but "domain and scale-dependent" in material mode (thesis 4). There is no single hierarchy of levels and no assumed fundamental level (theses 10–11, fn. 9). Ontology is effective and scale-relative (thesis 12). Scientific real patterns are lossy compressions, unlike the Game of Life's; the ideal gas laws use 3 degrees of freedom for ~10²³ (thesis 14). He quotes ETMG's Principle of Naturalistic Closure verbatim and calls it "problematic" because it refers to fundamental physics (p. 8). | Read |
| [NOTE-192](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-192.md) | Science, Metaphysics and Structural Realism | Ladyman argues that structural realism should be metaphysically, not epistemically, revisionary. He gives three grounds. Quantum statistics leaves the individuality of particles underdetermined: permutation invariance gives 3 two-particle states, not 4, so probabilities are 1/3 rather than 1/4 (p. 62). Entangled states give non-supervenient relations that refute Humean supervenience (pp. 71–73). The hole argument blocks point-based substantivalism (pp. 68–69). He proposes that science describes "the modal structure of the phenomena" (p. 73), with modal realism supplying the no-miracles intuition. The paper ends non-eliminatively: "the phenomena have structure but they are not structure" (p. 74). | Read |
| [NOTE-193](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-193.md) | Category-theoretic structure and radical ontic structural realism | Bain argues that the "relations without relata" objection to radical OSR depends on defining structure set-theoretically, where a relation is a subset of X × X. If a structure is instead "an object in a category", its elements (arrows 1 → A) are "not essential", because category-theoretic objects need not be structured sets. His examples are sheaves of Einstein algebras for GR with asymptotic boundary conditions, which may lack global sections (§3), and nCob and Hilb (§4). He concludes that ROSR "avoids the charge that it rests on an incoherent claim" (p. 1634). The paper never mentions the Yoneda lemma, representable functors or universal properties by name. | Read |
| [NOTE-194](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-194.md) | No categorial support for radical ontic structural realism | Lam and Wüthrich argue that category theory gives radical OSR no support. Identifying objects with identity arrows is relabelling (§3). Generalized elements X → A exist in every category, so elements are not eliminated (§4). Categories without products also lose set-style relations, "throwing out the relations with the relata" (§4). Morphisms relate C-objects, not the elements that stand for physical relata, and they "only distinguish isomorphism classes of structures" (§5). Bain's GR and TQFT cases eliminate at most spacetime points, not relata tout court (§§6–7). They conclude that category theory "offers little if any comfort to ROSR" (abstract) and favour "balanced" OSR. The Yoneda lemma is not named, although §§4–5 state two of its consequences. | Read |
| [NOTE-195](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-195.md) | Yoneda lemma | The page states that for a locally small category C, every presheaf X and every object c, there is a canonical bijection Hom_{[C^op,Set]}(y(c), X) ≅ X(c), given by η ↦ η_c(id_c). It proves the bijection by chasing id_c around a naturality square. From it the page derives that the Yoneda embedding y: c ↦ Hom_C(−,c) is fully faithful and that y(c) ≅ y(d) ⇔ c ≅ d. It also shows by counterexample that the hypothesis doing the work is naturality: objects whose hom-sets are pointwise in bijection need not be isomorphic. | Read |
| [NOTE-196](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-196.md) | Why Replication Is Overrated | Every experiment and every replication rests on individuation judgments about what is being manipulated and measured ("conceptual scope"). So direct replication can at most help rule out random error, and "perhaps" establish that some effect exists but not which effect, while conceptual replication begs the question by presupposing that two operationalisations individuate the same effect. What productive research actually does, as in the Mozart-effect debate, is probe systematic errors, which is better called exploration than replication. | Read |
| [NOTE-197](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-197.md) | A Pragmatist Theory of Evidence | Reiss proposes an "eliminativist hypothetico-contextualist" (EHC) theory of evidence for type-level causal claims in biomedical and social science. Support comes in two kinds: direct support (patterns we are "entitled to expect" if h is true) and indirect support (patterns incompatible with relevant alternatives). Warrant is graded by which alternatives have been eliminated, on four grades from "weak warrant" to "proof". Where to stop probing is set by context, meaning background knowledge, a default entitlement to other scientists' claims, and cost-benefit values. On this account there is no gold standard: RCTs are good only because they eliminate many alternatives at once. The 1950s smoking–lung cancer case illustrates the theory; it does not test it. | Read |
| [NOTE-198](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-198.md) | Minimum Description Length Revisited | MDL, reformulated around universal distributions and luckiness functions and presentable without coding theory, extends both penalized likelihood and Bayes, and unifies methods usually seen as rival — AIC vs BIC, cross-validation vs Bayes — within one worst-case framework. | Skimmed |
| [NOTE-199](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-199.md) | Balestriero & LeCun 2022 — SSL recovers spectral embedding | VICReg, SimCLR and Barlow Twins each solve, in closed form, a classical spectral embedding problem on the positive-pair graph G (Laplacian Eigenmaps, a generalized MDS/ISOMAP, and CCA respectively), so contrastive vs non-contrastive SSL is global vs local spectral embedding, and when G matches the downstream task any of them is optimal. | Skimmed |
| [NOTE-200](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-200.md) | Rate-distortion generalization bounds | Defining an algorithm's compressibility in source-coding terms shows that the Xu–Raginsky mutual-information bound is really a lossless compression rate, and allowing lossy compression yields bounds via the rate-distortion dimension that stay meaningful where I(S;W) is huge. | Skimmed |
| [NOTE-201](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-201.md) | Assran et al. 2022 — hidden uniform cluster prior in SSL | Joint-embedding SSL methods that rely on mini-batch volume maximization (SimCLR, VICReg, SwAV, MSN) implicitly impose a prior toward features that cluster the data into equal-sized groups, which hurts on class-imbalanced data; replacing it with a power-law prior (PMSN) improves representations on long-tailed data. | Skimmed |
| [NOTE-202](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-202.md) | Kolmogorov's structure functions and model selection | The structure function of a single data string determines all of its stochastic properties: within any complexity-constrained model class it picks out the best-fitting model with certainty. Every admissible shape of that function occurs for some data, and neither it nor the minimal sufficient statistic is computable. | Skimmed |
| [NOTE-203](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-203.md) | MDL induction, Bayesianism, and Kolmogorov complexity | Ideal MDL, derived from Bayes's rule with the universal prior, coincides with Bayesian MAP selection exactly when the data are random with respect to the hypothesis and the hypothesis is random with respect to the prior (the "Fundamental Inequality").
