Later lectures add Whitney's theorem, deletion–restriction, matroids and geometric lattices, broken circuits, supersolvability, the finite-field method, and the metric d(R, R′) = number of separating hyperplanes, whose zero set shows that a region is determined by its separating set (p. 89). | Read |
| [NOTE-314](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-314.md) | Calculation of signal detection theory measures | A practical tutorial that gives closed-form SDT measures computed from the hit rate H and false-alarm rate F. They are d′ = Φ⁻¹(H) − Φ⁻¹(F); β = exp{[Φ⁻¹(F)² − Φ⁻¹(H)²]/2}; c = −[Φ⁻¹(H) + Φ⁻¹(F)]/2; the nonparametric A′ and Grier's B″ (single-formula versions, eqs. 3 and 9); A_z = Φ(intercept/√(1+slope²)) from the z-ROC, whose slope is σ_noise/σ_signal; and A_d′ = Φ(d′/√2), which equals 2AFC proportion correct under the d′ assumptions. It also gives one-line commands for seven software packages. Its worked rating example (50 signal and 25 noise trials, noise SD larger than signal SD) shows d′ ranging from 0.84 to 1.86 across criteria. The authors conclude that d′ should not be used without rating-task evidence of equal variance. | Read |
| [NOTE-315](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-315.md) | The Theory of Signal Detectability. Part I: The General Theory; Part II: Applications with Gaussian Noise | Peterson and Birdsall show that the criterion approach (maximise P_SN(A) − β·P_N(A), or maximise detection at fixed false-alarm k) and the Woodward–Davies a-posteriori approach are all solved by one receiver that computes the likelihood ratio ℓ(x) = f_SN(x)/f_N(x) and thresholds it (Part I, Theorems 1–7). They introduce the receiver operating characteristic, whose slope at each point is the threshold β (Theorem 8, Eq. 2.51). For a signal known exactly in white Gaussian noise, ln ℓ is normal with equal variance 2E/N₀ under both hypotheses and means 2E/N₀ apart, so the ROC is a one-parameter family indexed by the "detection index" d = (M_SN − M_N)²/σ² = 2E/N₀ (Part II, Eqs. 4.1–4.8, Fig. 4.1). The receiver is a correlator or matched filter (Eq. 5.3, 4.10). | Skimmed |
| [NOTE-316](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-316.md) | A decision-making theory of visual detection | Tanner and Swets model visual detection as testing a statistical hypothesis. The observer compares one sample of neural activity with a cutoff, and signal+noise and noise are Gaussian with equal variance, separated by d′ (the mean difference in noise-SD units, defined as the square root of Peterson and Birdsall's d). The cutoff moves along a curve of P_SN(A) against P_N(A) (the curve now called the ROC; for d′ = 1 it passes through (.16, .50), (.50, .84) and (.84, .98)). The optimal operating point is where the slope equals β = [(1−P(SN))/P(SN)]·[(V_N·CA+K_N·A)/(V_SN·A+K_SN·CA)]. The 4AFC prediction is P(C)=∫F(x)³g(x)dx. With three paid observers, d′ estimated from yes-no data predicted 4AFC accuracy. The "false alarms are guesses" (high-threshold) hypothesis is rejected: false-alarm rate correlated with chance-corrected thresholds (ρ = .30, .71, .67; combined p ≪ .001), and all 12 fitted yes-no scatter lines miss the point (1, 1). | Read |
| [NOTE-317](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-317.md) | Grokking and Generalization Collapse: Insights from HTSR theory | A depth-3, width-200 ReLU MLP trained on 1,000 MNIST images (the Omnigrok recipe, with weights and biases scaled ×8, MSE loss, AdamW, WD = 0) for 10⁷ steps goes through three phases read off the accuracy curves. In pre-grokking (to ~10⁵ steps) training accuracy saturates while test accuracy stays low. In grokking (~10⁵–10⁶) test accuracy rises to ~0.85. In "anti-grokking" (~10⁶–10⁷) it falls to ~0.45–0.5 while training accuracy stays near 1. The average HTSR power-law exponent α of the two hidden weight matrices goes 4.0 → 2.9 → 1.1 at the phase ends (Table 1). In the collapse phase, correlation traps (outlier eigenvalues of the elementwise-shuffled weight matrix) appear. The paper's own weight-decay control shows FC2's α below 2, and traps in both layers, *without* a collapse (Fig. 6, Table 2). That undercuts its headline claim that α < 2 "invariably" heralds collapse. | Read |
| [NOTE-318](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-318.md) | New Evidence of the Two-Phase Learning Dynamics of Neural Networks | VGG-16 and ResNet-20 on CIFAR-10 (SGD with momentum, lr 0.1, 160 epochs) are compared across pairs of training times. Before an early "inflection point" (~2,500 iterations for VGG-16, ~100–500 for ResNet-20), a tiny parameter perturbation under identical SGD noise sends the run to a different basin by the end: loss barrier up to ~2, test disagreement ~0.15–0.5. After it, the same perturbation leaves the runs linearly connected. Also after it, the empirical NTK keeps changing step to step but stays within a bounded cosine distance of any later reference kernel (the "cone effect", e.g. S ≈ 0.05–0.1 for VGG-16 vs ≈ 0.15–0.2 from initialisation). Switching to linearised training later gives better test accuracy (VGG-16 0.13 → 0.82 as the switch moves from ≤200 to 5,000 iterations). | Read |
| [NOTE-319](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-319.md) | How do language models learn facts? Dynamics, curricula and hallucinations | 'An 8-layer, 44M-parameter decoder-only transformer trained with AdamW on synthetic biographies (64k individuals by default) learns factual recall in three phases. It first fits the marginal attribute-value distribution, then sits on a plateau exactly at the no-knowledge baseline, then acquires individual-specific associations. Plateau end grows with population as 0.43·N^0.81 (R² = 0.998, N = 4k–256k, 5 seeds). Attention patching from post-plateau checkpoints removes the plateau, and attention from the recall position to name tokens rises through it, so the plateau is when the attention extraction circuit forms. Nothing in the three phases is a compression of input information: the last phase adds individual-specific information.' | Read |
| [NOTE-320](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-320.md) | On the Information Bottleneck Theory of Deep Learning | Replicating Shwartz-Ziv & Tishby with their own code, the paper shows the information-plane "compression phase" appears only for double-saturating nonlinearities measured through an imposed binning/noise model: tanh compresses, ReLU/softplus/linear do not (binning, KDE, Kraskov; SZT task and MNIST), and re-binning the same tanh run evenly in net input removes it. Compression and generalisation dissociate in all four combinations, full-batch GD compresses as much as SGD, and the drift→diffusion gradient-SNR transition occurs in networks that cannot compress (ReLU, MNIST, a 1-1-1 linear net), so it is real but unrelated to compression. | Read |
| [NOTE-321](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-321.md) v3 | The two dragons of cognition: recursive condensation for predictive processing | A Hypothesis-and-Theory essay, the cognitive statement of Li's structural-learning programme. Intelligence pays once for exploratory search, condenses the result into stable discrete tokens, and then navigates cheaply. The cortex and the sleep rhythms are proposed as this cycle's implementation (gamma as the expand/contract clock, the laminar circuit, STDP, SWRs nested in Slow-4). Charitably read, Savitch stands for memoisation and Urysohn for "collapsed, separated classes admit a simple readout". On that reading the core is a coherent restatement of amortised inference and complementary learning systems, with untested and largely non-discriminating predictions. The headline scaling law, linear cortex giving exponential reach (Theorem 2), is stated without argument, and the claim that collapse defeats the curse of dimensionality leaves the cost of learning the collapse unaddressed. | Read |
| [NOTE-322](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-322.md) v2 | Structural Decoupling: A Scaffold-Flow Theory of Generalization and Alignment | Li's structural learning theory (StrLT, developed in the companion arXiv 2602.07974) splits multi-regime learning into discovering contexts (the "trap", or scaffold) and predicting within one (the "funnel", or flow). Width is the least number of cells on which a γ-contractive, low-risk predictor suffices. It is incomparable with the per-cell VC dimension, forces an error floor when fewer than w cells are allocated, and costs Ω(w log w) samples to discover. These results hold in repaired form, and are close to definitional. The CS estimator reweights a spatial graph by prediction agreement. It is shown to count components for a given predictor, not width itself. The decoupling prescription says to train routing on structural signals, not task loss, and to freeze consolidated contexts. It is a plausible, testable design hypothesis with continual-learning precedent, but the additive Rademacher bound it is said to follow from holds for joint training too. The safety section recasts alignment as scaffold alignment by analogy, and is asserted. | Read |
| [NOTE-323](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-323.md) | Local Urysohn Width: A Topological Complexity Measure for Classification | >- It defines local Urysohn width uw_{D₀}(P, γ): the least number of connected sets of diameter ≤ D₀, each carrying a continuous classifier correct on the margin-γ safe region it meets, needed to cover that safe region. It proves: - uw = w on a bouquet of w circles with safe balls at the antipodes, when 3γ/2 ≤ D₀ < L/2 − 3γ/4 (Thm 4.1); - uw ≥ w·m, with m = Θ(L/D₀) safe balls per loop, so uw = Ω(w·L/D₀) (Cor 5.1); - two-way non-determination with VC dimension (Thm 6.1); - an Ω(w log w) sample lower bound via label-permuted coupon collection (Thm 7.1); - uw ≥ 2β₁/Δ₀ for good, convex, bounded-adjacency covers (Thm 8.5). The proofs are correct. The obstruction is metric, as the paper concedes in Remark 8.11: patches cannot reach across the wedge point. Because Urysohn's lemma makes local correctness automatic on any patch, the measure is a connected covering number of the safe region that does not depend on the labels. | Read |
| [NOTE-324](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-324.md) | Structural Learning Theory: A Metric-Topology Factorization Approach | >- The formal core of Xin Li's structural-learning programme. Width w(P; γ, δ) is the least number of open cells on each of which some predictor is γ-contractive with conditional risk ≤ δ. The paper proves that width is incomparable with the per-cell VC dimension (Thm 2.2). It shows that with K < w cells there is an n-independent error floor η(w, K), under a non-degeneracy assumption stated in the body (Thm 2.4, Assumption 2.3). Width is at least c·β₁·L/D₀ under a bounded-overlap assumption (Thm 2.5), and discovering all basins costs Ω(w log w) samples (Thm 2.6). The CS-Laplacian eigenvalue count converges uniformly to the predictor-relative count w_G(P) when n ≳ (H_G + d_X log(1/r_x) + log(1/δ′))/(r_x^{d_X} g_eff²) (Thm 3.5); no result shows that w_G equals width. There are no experiments. | Read |
| [NOTE-325](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-325.md) | On Context-Content Uncertainty Principle | The paper assumes structured content has far lower entropy than context, H(Φ) ≪ H(Ψ), and from the chain rule H(Φ,Ψ) = H(Φ) + H(Ψ\|Φ) concludes that inference should establish content first and then resolve context. It then derives four layers of "operational principles" and a reading of five brain theories as instances. The principles cover attention, learning rates, memory, staged learning and hierarchy. All of this is argued, not shown. There are no experiments, the abstract's simulations are absent, and the proofs of Theorems 1, 3 and 6–8 are wrong as written. | Read |
| [NOTE-326](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-326.md) | 'What is a Thing?': Topos Theory in the Foundations of Physics | The Döring–Isham programme in one volume. A theory of a system is a representation of a typed language L(S) in a topos. In quantum theory, presheaves over the abelian von Neumann subalgebras V(H), daseinisation sends projectors injectively to clopen sub-objects of the spectral presheaf Σ, preserving ∨ but not ∧. A pure state gives sieve-valued truth values. Every bounded self-adjoint Â becomes an arrow Σ → ℝ^↔ whose value at a point is a pair of monotone functions, an interval that widens as contexts coarsen. Most of it reprints papers I–IV. New are the inverse-image corollary linking PL(S) and L(S), values in a pseudo-state, and the algebra of the quantity-value candidates. The Heyting structure on Sub_cl(Σ) is still only sketched. | Read |
| [NOTE-327](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-327.md) | A Topos Perspective on the Kochen-Specker Theorem: III. Von Neumann Algebras as the Base Category | Hamilton, Isham & Butterfield move the series' base category from operators (O) and Boolean subalgebras (W) to the poset V of commutative von Neumann subalgebras of B(H). There they restate Kochen–Specker as "the spectral presheaf Σ(V) = Gelfand spectrum of V has no global elements" (dim H > 2, cited, §2.2). They identify the coarse-graining presheaf G(V) = L(V), G(i)(P̂) = inf{Q̂ ∈ L(V₂) \| P̂ ≤ Q̂} (Def. 3.1), with the presheaf CloΣ of clopen subsets of the spectra; the isomorphism is asserted, and part IV proves it. A state ρ yields the sieve-valued valuation ν^ρ_V(P̂) = {V′ ⊆ V \| ρ(G(P̂)) = 1} (Def. 3.4) and the support projectors inf{P̂ ∈ L(V) \| ρ(P̂) = 1}. These form a global element of G and hence an "interval valuation", a subobject of Σ (§§4.2–4.4). The probability-r valuations give subobjects of G but not global elements. | Read |
| [NOTE-328](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-328.md) | A Topos Perspective on the Kochen-Specker Theorem: IV. Interval Valuations | Butterfield & Isham show, over the poset V of commutative von Neumann subalgebras, that sieve-valued valuations and "interval valuations" (assignments of support projectors, or of subsets of Gelfand spectra) determine each other under stated conditions. If supports s(α, V) = inf{P̂ \| α_V(P̂) = true_V} form a global element of the coarse-graining presheaf G, then α_{V₁}(P̂) = {V₂ ⊆ V₁ \| G(s(α, V₁)) ≤ G(P̂)} is a sieve-valued valuation obeying FUNC (Theorem 3.1). There is an analogue for "tight" subobjects of Σ (Theorem 3.2). Both are satisfied by every state's valuation ν^ρ. The ordering ≤ (or ⊆) is shown to be the natural relation securing sievehood, null, monotonicity, exclusivity and unit (§4). The review §2.4 also supplies the proof, via state extension, that G ≅ CloΣ: restricting the clopen set of P̂ to V₂ gives the clopen set of its coarse-graining. Part III had only asserted this. | Read |
| [NOTE-329](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-329.md) | A Topos Perspective on the Kochen-Specker Theorem: II. Conceptual Aspects, and Classical Analogues | Butterfield & Isham argue that the sieve-valued "generalised valuations" of part I are not a quantum peculiarity but the natural valuations for any presheaf of propositions on any small category. Three supports are offered. (1) Classical physics: the value presheaf Υ(Ā) = Ā(S) on the category M of measurable functions has global sections (one per microstate, eq. 3.7), yet a macrostate R ⊆ S yields a sieve-valued valuation ν^R(A ∈ Δ) = {f_M \| B̄(R) ⊆ f(Δ)} (eq. 3.10) with the same FUNC property as the quantum ν^ρ. (2) A proof that a sieve-valued valuation on any presheaf G obeys generalised FUNC, ν(B, G(f)(d)) = f*(ν(A, d)), iff it is a natural transformation G → Ω, i.e. a subobject of G (Theorem 4.1). (3) An informal argument, explicitly "not a genuine deduction" (§5.2), that if partial truth is fixed by which weakenings of a proposition are totally true, the truth value is a sieve. | Read |
| [NOTE-330](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-330.md) | Damasio & Carvalho — The nature of feelings | An Opinion with no new data. Feelings are the felt side of interoceptive body maps, and they report homeostatic value. Feeling does not need the insula: the authors' own patient with both insulae destroyed still feels. It needs the upper brainstem, where whole-body maps first assemble. The evolutionary story (felt maps beat unfelt ones because they support learning and anticipation) and the cellular one (unmyelinated, ligand- and ion-permeable interoceptive axons, with ephaptic coupling, as the root of sentience) are hypotheses, and are presented as such. | Read |
| [NOTE-331](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-331.md) | Bechara, Damasio, Tranel & Damasio — Deciding advantageously before knowing the advantageous strategy | Ten controls and six ventromedial prefrontal patients play the four-deck gambling task while anticipatory skin-conductance responses (SCRs) and verbal reports are tracked. Every control and no patient develops anticipatory SCRs; three patients who can state the right strategy still choose badly. Controls prefer the good decks significantly only from the hunch period, and the pre-hunch SCR effect is a group-by-period interaction, not a deck difference, so "before knowing" is better supported for articulated strategy than for any awareness at all. | Read |
| [NOTE-332](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-332.md) | Damasio — The somatic marker hypothesis and the possible functions of the prefrontal cortex | The somatic marker hypothesis as its author stated it in 1996. Ventromedial prefrontal cortex stores links between classes of situation and the body states that accompanied them; on recurrence it re-enacts those states, via the body or an "as if" loop, and the re-enacted state marks imagined option-outcome pairs as good or bad, overtly as a feeling or covertly as a bias, pruning the space reasoning must search. The evidence is summarised from earlier lesion and skin-conductance studies, without numbers. The mechanism is argued, not shown, and the paper says it is not a theory of prefrontal cortex as a whole. | Read |
| [NOTE-333](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-333.md) | Topos Theory and Consistent Histories: The Internal Logic of the Set of All Consistent Sets | Isham takes the poset B of all Boolean subalgebras ("windows") of the history orthoalgebra UP, ordered by coarse-graining, and shows that the presheaf A_d(W) = {α \| some d-consistent W′ ⊇ W contains α} is a subobject of the constant presheaf ΔUP, whereas the naive "realisable in W" assignment is not a presheaf at all (§4.1–4.2). The probability claim ⟨α, p⟩ then gets the sieve-valued semantic value {W ⊆ W₀ \| ∃ d-consistent W′ ⊇ W with α ∈ W′} if d(α, α) = p and ∅ otherwise (4.16), a global element 1 → Ω in Set^B, so the logic of "all consistent sets at once" is the distributive but non-Boolean Heyting algebra of sieves on B. | Read |
| [NOTE-334](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-334.md) | Some Possible Roles for Topos Theory in Quantum Theory and Quantum Gravity | Isham & Butterfield argue, without proving anything new, that presheaf toposes over a base of "contexts" suit physics because the subobject classifier Ω is fixed by the base. They say this answers the objection that many-valued logics are arbitrary (§2.2), and that it keeps the logic distributive (§1.1). They then sketch seven applications. Five are speculative one-paragraph proposals (frames, observers, causal sets, TQFT, synthetic-differential-geometry time in continuous histories). One (§3.7) recaps their Kochen–Specker results: KS ⇔ the dual presheaf D(Â) = Hom(W_A, {0,1}) on the operator category O has no global sections (dim H > 2), and every state ψ gives a FUNC-obeying sieve-valued valuation ν^ψ(A ∈ Δ) = {f_O : Â → B̂ \| Ê[B ∈ f(Δ)]ψ = ψ}. | Read |
| [NOTE-335](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-335.md) | Putnam — Psychological Predicates | Pain is hypothesised to be a functional state of a whole organism, a probabilistic automaton's state fixed by its machine table, rather than a physical-chemical brain state, because a functional organisation common to all pain-feelers is a likelier invariant than a physical one. The hypothesis carries the anti-nesting condition (3): no pain-feeler has a decomposition into parts that separately possess the pain-capable kind of Description. Its only stated motive is to exclude swarms of bees. Its clause (1) is empty, since everything is a probabilistic automaton under some Description. | Skimmed |
| [NOTE-336](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-336.md) | Chalmers — Facing Up to the Problem of Consciousness | The easy problems of consciousness are problems of explaining functions, which a mechanism explains; the hard problem is that "why is the performance of these functions accompanied by experience?" survives the explanation of every function. The argument is conceptual: the easy phenomena are functionally definable and experience is not. The positive part takes experience as fundamental and proposes structural coherence and organizational invariance as nonbasic psychophysical principles, with a double-aspect theory of information as a speculative basic one. Nothing is measured; the invariance argument is a one-page sketch. | Read |
| [NOTE-337](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-337.md) | A Framework for Representing Knowledge | The frame memo. A frame is a stereotyped situation-structure whose terminals carry conditions and weakly bound defaults. Frames sharing terminals form systems whose transformations encode moves, actions and shifts of viewpoint, and mismatches are routed through a learned similarity network. Applied to vision, imagery, language, memory, search and control, with an appendix arguing that monotonic, consistency-seeking logic cannot carry commonsense knowledge. A programme statement: nothing is built or measured, and the author says what is missing. | Read |
| [NOTE-338](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-338.md) | Where Am I? | A first-person story with an argument inside. With his brain in a vat in Houston radio-linked to his body under Tulsa, the narrator tests three answers to "where am I?": where the body is, where the brain is, where the point of view is. He favours the third and finds it unclear. A computer duplicate of his brain can take over his body with no detectable difference, and when duplicate and brain drift out of step there are two persons in one body. Intuition pumps, no thesis defended to the end. | Read |
| [NOTE-339](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-339.md) | Examining the Society of Mind | An exposition, read in full. Singh dates the Society of Mind to Minsky and Papert's early-1970s robot work, finds its agent in the procedure-bearing frame of 1974, and quotes the unpublished 1976 book drafts. He sorts the 1986 book's mechanisms into agents, simplest agents, agency construction, problem solving, communication and growth. Agents need no shared code: K-lines, randomly wired buses and paranomes stand in for messages. He finds near-relatives in Bayes nets, case-based reasoning, blackboards, Cyc and Soar, and concludes that the theory has never been implemented. Its claims of lineage are asserted, not shown. | Read |
| [NOTE-340](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-340.md) | List — What is it like to be a group agent? | A short, conditional argument. Group agents are real by a thin functionalist definition and a naturalistic indispensability argument, and they have consciousness as awareness. Phenomenal consciousness turns on a bridge principle. Agency-, biology- and information-based principles are surveyed, and IIT is adopted. Three structural reasons (functional decomposability, robustness of steering bodies to partial randomisation, processing done mainly inside members) are said to make a group's Φ low. The exclusion postulate is a second route. Nothing is computed. The result is used to ground a normative asymmetry: group agents have only derivative rights. | Read |
| [NOTE-341](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-341.md) | The Computational Boundary of a "Self" | A hypothesis essay. A Self is the set of parts that pursue goals together, and its boundary is the farthest region of space and time it can measure and act on, its "cognitive light cone". Selves grow from homeostatic loops by adding memory, anticipation and networks, and cells form larger Selves by sharing bioelectric state through gap junctions. Every level that has its own goal is a Self, so living systems hold many nested Selves at once; the paper says this against IIT in one bracket. It has no model, no measurement of the light cone and no new data, and it confines claims about experience to a closing speculation. | Read |
| [NOTE-342](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-342.md) | Chalmers — Does a rock implement every finite-state automaton? | Implementation needs strong, counterfactual-supporting conditionals for every transition in the table, not one run, so Putnam's rock does not implement every FSA. Repaired, the triviality result survives for weak formalisms. A clock plus a dial implements every inputless FSA. The right input–output dependencies plus an input memory and a dial implement every FSA with those dependencies. Combinatorial-state automata, with independently varying components in distinct physical regions, demand fine-grained causal structure, and a blind implementation must grow exponentially. Logically possible false implementations and an overstrict independence condition are left open. Minds attach to (system, CSA) pairs, so the Chinese room holds two. | Read |
| [NOTE-343](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-343.md) | Rupert — Challenges to the Hypothesis of Extended Cognition | Rupert separates extended cognition (HEC) from embedded cognition (HEMC) and from content externalism, then argues that HEC loses to HEMC on conservatism. [LIT-097](../../record/literature.d/LIT-097.md)'s criteria give a dilemma: without past endorsement they overgenerate beliefs; with it the organism is privileged. Memory research (conversation, negative transfer, the generation effect, power laws) shows external stores are a different kind, and a generic kind spanning both does no explanatory work. | Read |
| [NOTE-344](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-344.md) | Plain Talk About Neurodevelopmental Epistemology | The first worked mechanism of the society-of-agents view, then "The Society of Minds". Agents too simple for language exchange no messages: each argument sits at a fixed location on shared channels (c-lines), and the agents themselves are short-term memory. A specificity gradient makes low-level communities local, persistence-memory and a case-shift handle interruptions without a stack, and the infant's internal "cognitive cases" are conjectured to precede and shape linguistic cases. Speculation throughout; the author says so. | Read |
| [NOTE-345](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-345.md) | Chalmers — Could a large language model be conscious? | Asks for a feature X that indicates LLM consciousness or its absence. Self-report is fragile and trained in. Conversation and general intelligence give some limited reason. Six obstacles stand against it: biology, senses and embodiment, world and self models, recurrence, a global workspace and unified agency, the last three strongest, all but biology temporary. On mainstream assumptions, with at least 1/3 credence per requirement, that gives under 10% for current LLMs and 25% or more for conscious LLM+ systems within a decade. The numbers are illustrative, biology is set aside rather than answered, and the twelve "challenges" are offered as roadmap or red flags. | Read |
| [NOTE-346](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-346.md) | Dennett — Facing Backwards on the Problem of Consciousness | A three-page reply to Chalmers's "Facing Up". The easy/hard split misdirects research: a vitalist could pose the same hard problem of life, and a hard problem of perception set aside from its "easy" parts would be an artifact. Chalmers's reason why experience differs restates the claim. Subtracting every function from one's own consciousness leaves nothing but a conviction. Adding experience to physics' fundamentals would need independent evidence, which is absent. Argument by analogy, with one introspective premise. | Read |
| [NOTE-347](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-347.md) | Chalmers — Absent Qualia, Fading Qualia, Dancing Qualia | Organizational invariance, that fine-grained functional isomorphs of a conscious system have the same experiences, is defended by reductio over gradual neuron replacement. Absent qualia would imply a rational being systematically wrong about its own fading experience. Inverted or absent qualia would imply experience that flips with a switch and cannot be noticed. Both are judged implausible, not impossible. The paper extends the argument to a population of homunculi and to a single record-keeping homunculus. The conclusion is natural, not metaphysical, supervenience of experience on organisation, and it says nothing about systems that are not isomorphs. | Read |
| [NOTE-348](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-348.md) | The Emotion Machine | Read in the 2005 draft. Emotions are Ways to Think: each emotional state is a selection of mental resources turned on and off, made by Selectors that Critics trigger when they recognise a type of problem, and ordinary thinking uses the same switching. On this design sit six levels of mental activity, panalogies that link descriptions across realms, and a Self that is a network of partial models. It is argued by example and analogy, with no experiment and few neural claims. Against Damasio, the body is a resource the brain exploits, not what an emotion is made of; on Elliot it proposes, without evidence, the reverse causal direction. | Read |
| [NOTE-349](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-349.md) | Searle — Minds, brains, and programs | A man who follows English rules for manipulating Chinese symbols can answer Chinese questions like a native speaker without understanding Chinese, so no program is by itself sufficient for understanding. Six replies are answered; the systems reply by having the man internalise the room. Searle grants that machines could think, if they had the brain's causal powers. The argument concerns intentionality, and its answer to the systems reply rests on treating the internalised system as a part of the man, which is the point in dispute. | Read |
| [NOTE-350](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-350.md) | Intentional Systems | Defines an Intentional system as anything whose behaviour can be predicted by ascribing beliefs and desires and assuming rationality, and makes the status relative to the predictor's strategy and indifferent to the system's matter and consciousness. Argues that Intentional theory is vacuous as psychology because it presupposes rationality, that behaviourism smuggles it in, and that every content-ascribing theory owes a "loan of intelligence" that must be repaid by analysing away a little man. Ends on belief's two norms, truth and accurate avowal. | Read |
| [NOTE-351](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-351.md) | Intelligence without representation | A methodological manifesto with working robots behind it. Brooks argues that AI should build complete autonomous Creatures incrementally and test them in the real world, and that intelligence should be decomposed into parallel activity-producing layers, each running from sensing to action, not into perception, central reasoning and action. His robots have no central representation or control, and their coherence exists "in the eye of an observer", as in Minsky's account of human behaviour. The evidence is three layers on one robot, described, not measured, and the broad hypothesis about representation is flagged as a hypothesis. | Read |
| [NOTE-352](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-352.md) | K-Lines: A Theory of Memory | Memory as reinstatement: a K-line, attached on a memorable event to the agents then active, later re-imposes that partial mental state. The level-band principle limits it to an intermediate band of levels, so the present is seen as an instance of the past without re-imposing the old answer. K-lines build on earlier K-lines into a K-pyramid against the perceptual P-pyramid; cross-exclusion makes conflicting details cancel into abstraction; learning needs a third, goal-holding net. A programme statement: no simulation, and the P→K link is left unspecified. | Read |
| [NOTE-353](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-353.md) | True Believers: The Intentional Strategy and Why It Works | Belief is objective but visible only through a strategy: a true believer is any system "reliably and voluminously predictable" by treating it as a rational agent with the beliefs and desires it ought to have. Adopting the stance is free; its success is a fact, and Laplacean Martians who skipped it would miss real patterns. Thermostats and people differ in degree, by how tightly their states are tied to one interpretation. Evolution explains easily why the strategy works; how the machinery does it is open, and the language of thought is a bet, not a necessity. | Read |
| [NOTE-354](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-354.md) | Chalmers — The Meta-Problem of Consciousness | The meta-problem, why we judge that consciousness poses a hard problem, is an easy problem to be solved in topic-neutral terms. The paper surveys thirteen mechanisms and favours introspective models that attribute primitive relations of acquaintance to primitive qualities. It then poses a meta-problem challenge: a theory's basis of consciousness should explain our judgments about consciousness, which IIT and biological theories struggle to meet. It blocks debunking arguments for illusionism, but concedes a residual coincidence. It argues that only strong illusionism dissolves the hard problem, and rejects it by a Moorean argument. Nothing is measured; the solution offered is an explanandum sketch. | Read |
| [NOTE-355](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-355.md) | Turing — Computing Machinery and Intelligence | Turing replaces "Can machines think?" by whether a digital computer can play the imitation game, predicts that by about 2000 a 10^9-bit machine will hold an average interrogator to 70 per cent right identifications after five minutes, and answers nine objections. The answer to the argument from consciousness is that its extreme form is solipsism. The positive case is admitted to be weak ("recitations tending to produce belief"); the programme is to educate a child-machine. | Read |
| [NOTE-356](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-356.md) | Chalmers — Extended Cognition and Extended Consciousness | Chalmers restates the 1998 extended-mind thesis as a claim about constitution through sensorimotor interaction, because the original statement is satisfied by wired-in "extended circuits" that the critics accept. He keeps the perception-action boundary as the strongest objection, and argues that consciousness does not extend because it needs information directly available for global control, and perception makes availability indirect. Both moves are proposed and defended against a few cases, not demonstrated. | Read |
| [NOTE-357](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-357.md) | Dennett — Quining Qualia | Qualia are supposed to be ineffable, intrinsic, private and directly apprehensible properties of experience. Fifteen intuition pumps argue that the pretheoretical concept is confused and that nothing has all four marks. The core move: an inversion in early processing and an inversion in memory-linked reactions are introspectively indistinguishable, so a subject has no privileged access to which happened. In place of qualia the paper offers practically ineffable, idiosyncratically detected public properties. It is argument by example throughout, and the author says so. | Read |
| [NOTE-358](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-358.md) | Chalmers — The combination problem for panpsychism | A taxonomy, not a solution. The combination problem is three problems (subject, quality, structure) plus four smaller ones (unity, boundary, awareness, grain). Each yields an argument against constitutive panpsychism, and subject-summing and structural mismatch are the strongest. Responses fall into emergent, autonomous, identity and combinatorial panpsychism. The non-combinatorial ones pay in mental causation or revised physics. Phenomenal bonding, quantum holism, small palettes and informational composition are judged most promising, but none is shown to work. James's argument at its origin is an argument against group minds, and a footnote objects to IIT's exclusion postulate that it makes consciousness extrinsic. | Read |
| [NOTE-359](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-359.md) | High-level perception, representation, and analogy: A critique of artificial intelligence methodology | Traditional AI models of discovery and analogy (BACON, the Structure-Mapping Engine, ACME) start from representations built by people who already know the answer, and so skip high-level perception, where most of the difficulty lies. Two informal arguments say the gap cannot be closed later by a separate representation module: analogy shapes perception, and the right representation depends on the task. Copycat is described as an architecture that builds representations and mappings together. Argued well; nothing is measured in the paper. | Read |
| [NOTE-360](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-360.md) | Selfridge — Pandemonium | A proposal, with a preliminary Morse-code trial, for pattern recognition by a hierarchy of parallel "demons". Data demons hold the input, subdemons compute features, cognitive demons shout weighted sums, and a decision demon takes the loudest. It adapts by hill-climbing on the weights, by culling and breeding subdemons through mutation and "conjugation", and in principle by adapting its own control demons. The only result is reported in the discussion: hill-climbing "does work", subdemon selection "did not do what we hoped", and the best single subdemon was right 90% of the time. | Read |
| [NOTE-361](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-361.md) | Jokes and the Logic of the Cognitive Unconscious | Humour as the teaching and engaging of "censors": learned, unconscious suppressors of thought-paths known to fail (paradox, circling, false analogy, prohibited wishes). Commonsense logic is too unreliable to repair, general methods can only be corrected subtractively, so minds accumulate negative meta-knowledge, and jokes, built on unexpected frame-substitution, are how it is learned. This explains why logical nonsense is funny in the same way as Freud's forbidden subjects. An essay of conjectures, with no data. | Read |
| [NOTE-362](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-362.md) v2 | Kammerer — How a materialist can deny that the US is probably conscious | The Sophisticated Anti-Nesting Principle. A whole W with a functional property P that would normally suffice for conscious state S lacks S if some part of W plays a role that (A) requires the part to have conscious states representing W and (B) is needed for W to have P (or any other property sufficient for S). The SAP leaves the antheads and Block's tiny-people brain conscious, permits some group consciousness, and denies the US conscious states because its citizens represent it and could not be replaced by parts that do not. Its defence is an Occam-style rule against ascribing consciousness where other subjects' states explain the behaviour, plus a tu quoque on residual arbitrariness. | Read |
| [NOTE-363](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-363.md) | Clark — Intrinsic content, active memory and the extended mind | Clark defends [LIT-097](../../record/literature.d/LIT-097.md) against Adams and Aizawa's intrinsic-content objection: the notion is unclear, silicon could carry it, and their own concession that not every cognitive state needs non-derived content lets a conventional encoding serve as a belief's vehicle. Against the active-memory objection, he concedes the notebook is static and argues that how and when memory is updated is a difference below the level that matters. He closes on brains as open-ended controllers and persons as forensic, not biological, items. | Read |
| [NOTE-364](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-364.md) | The Self as a Center of Narrative Gravity | A self is an abstractum like a centre of gravity, posited by interpretation, and like a fictional character it is indeterminate beyond what its story fixes. A mindless novel-writing robot can author a self whose story is true of the robot. Brains are bundles of partly autonomous systems that make themselves cohere into one autobiography, and when the story coheres around two points there are two selves. The essay argues by analogy and thought experiment. Its evolutionary account of self-talk is speculation, and its evidence is cited. | Read |
| [NOTE-365](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-365.md) | Integrated Information Theory 3.0 | The 2014 restatement of IIT. Five axioms of experience become five postulates about mechanisms in a state, and an experience is identified with the maximally irreducible conceptual structure of a complex. Exclusion is the anti-nesting rule: of all overlapping sets of elements, only the one with locally maximal Φ is conscious, so no subset or superset of a complex is. It is postulated from the axiom that experience has definite borders; Occam's razor is invoked only for exclusion within a mechanism. Logic-gate examples give minor complexes, a conscious inactive network, a minimally conscious photodiode and a feed-forward zombie. Nothing larger than about a dozen elements is computable. | Read |
| [NOTE-366](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-366.md) | Block — Troubles with Functionalism | Functionalism fixes mental states only by their relations to inputs and outputs, so a body run by a billion radio-linked people, each executing one line of your machine table, is functionally equivalent to you. There is prima facie doubt that it has qualia, and Block argues the doubt has a rational basis: it is unlike us in psychology and neurophysiology, and it was built to mimic us. The doubt does not depend on the parts having minds. Putnam's anti-nesting stipulation is an ad hoc patch that fails in every version Block tries. Psychofunctionalism secures beliefs but perhaps not qualia, and no account of inputs and outputs avoids both liberalism and chauvinism. | Read |
| [NOTE-367](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-367.md) | Dennett & Kinsbourne — Time and the Observer | The Cartesian Theater is the tacit assumption that some place or moment in the brain makes contents conscious, so that arrival order there fixes experienced order. The Multiple Drafts model replaces it: discriminations are made once, locally, and revised in parallel; temporal order is represented by content, not by the timing of vehicles. Four timing "anomalies" then need no backward projection. At sub-second scales the difference between pre- and post-experiential revision is said to be only verbal. It is a conceptual argument over published data; no experiment is run. | Read |
| [NOTE-368](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-368.md) | Adams & Aizawa — The bounds of cognition | Adams and Aizawa grant [LIT-097](../../record/literature.d/LIT-097.md)'s parity sentence but deny that any actual case meets it. Cognition, they assume, needs non-derived content and a distinctive kind of causal process. Otto's inscriptions have only derived content, Otto's retrieval uses vision and motor control that Inga's does not, and coupling a process to its surroundings does not extend it. So cognition is brain-bound as a contingent fact, and human-plus-tool systems are too motley for a science. | Read |
| [NOTE-369](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-369.md) | Chalmers — Foreword to Supersizing the Mind | Ten years after [LIT-097](../../record/literature.d/LIT-097.md), its co-author says the iPhone has made the thesis vivid, that the only principled objection is the perception-action boundary, and that the right reply is to let belief-ascription follow explanatory purpose, so that Otto's belief is extended for some explanations and not others. Consciousness probably stays internal, because no twin case for it can be built. The thesis needs only a weak parity-style functionalism. | Read |
| [NOTE-370](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-370.md) | Shanahan, McDonell & Reynolds — Role play with large language models (read for the self) | Read for the self. The simulator has no beliefs, goals or agency, "not even simulated versions"; simulacra only role-play characters that have them, so when an agent says "I" there is "no-one at home". The paper names, and does not answer, the question of which self a dialogue agent would act to preserve: hardware, process or instance. The denial of a self follows from the framing; no argument rules out a conscious actor behind the roles. | Read |
| [NOTE-371](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-371.md) | Dennett — Reply to Professor Stich | Dennett accepts Stich's summary of Brainstorms and answers his three charges. The evolutionary grounding of rationality is deliberately minimal. Full rationality is an idealisation, irrationality has no stable description, and so personhood rests on a concept "we only imperfectly achieve". On pain, eliminativism and the reality of suffering are reconciled by policy: biting the bullet, reform and common sense each have uses, and Dennett stays with "there are no such things as pains, although of course people do feel pain". | Read |
| [NOTE-372](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-372.md) | Self models | Metzinger's compact statement of the self-model theory of subjectivity. No substantial self exists; there is a self-model, partly conscious (the PSM), used for global control. A phenomenal self exists if and only if the PSM is transparent; a first-person perspective is a transparent model of the intentionality relation. Self-models can be unconscious and artificial, and embodiment comes in three orders. The central claim is stated with a prediction, not argued; the examples are cited. The online text has editing slips and reference gaps. | Read |
| [NOTE-373](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-373.md) | Survival and Identity | What matters in survival is the R-relation (mental continuity and connectedness among person-stages), and it is also identity, because persons are maximal R-interrelated aggregates of stages and so the relation of being stages of one person is the R-relation. Fission and fusion become persons sharing stages, counted by tensed identity; the long-lived Methuselah is a continuum of overlapping persons; degrees come from supervaluation over cut-offs. Against Perry's lifetimes, two persons, not three, take part in a fission. | Read |
| [NOTE-374](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-374.md) | Personal Identity | A survey of the persistence debate with an animalist's emphasis. It separates seven questions, argues that the persistence question concerns numerical identity and must not presuppose person essentialism, and sets out psychological-continuity, brute-physical and anticriterialist answers. Fission and the too-many-thinkers objection are the two arguments that do the work. It avoids the word "self". One in-text citation (Olson and Witt 2020) has no matching bibliography entry. | Read |
| [NOTE-375](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-375.md) | Burns — Fluid Concepts and Creative Analogies: A Review | A three-page review of Hofstadter and FARG's collection. It explains the parallel terraced scan and "cognition is recognition" clearly, then asks why the work has had little impact on AI: microdomains with possibly hidden assumptions, and critiques of rivals that rest on analogy and intuition and are not turned on Copycat. Its sharpest point is that the symbol-grounding charge against SME applies to Copycat too. | Read |
| [NOTE-376](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-376.md) | Shanahan — Palatable Conceptions of Disembodied Being | An avowedly "poetic" attempt to fit consciousness and selfhood to a disembodied 2024 chat agent. Its time would be discrete and discontinuous. Its "I" can be the model or a conversation-bound self that flickers into being each turn; we may slip between them. Such selves can be copied, edited and merged, and spread over branching simulacra. No candidate site survives scrutiny, which is read as Buddhist emptiness and applied back to human selves. Examples are invented usages, not data. | Read |
| [NOTE-377](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-377.md) | Philosophical conceptions of the self: implications for cognitive science | A short review that sets the minimal self (present self-consciousness) against the narrative self (extended in time), splits the minimal self into a sense of ownership and a sense of agency, and ties their dissociation in schizophrenic thought insertion to Frith's forward comparator model. It reports an embodied minimal self in neonatal imitation and a momentary one in Strawson and in Tani's robot, and proposes, against Dennett, that the narrative self is the sum of its narratives. Evidence is cited, not shown. | Read |
| [NOTE-378](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-378.md) | Personal Identity | Wiggins's division case shows that what matters in survival need not be one-one, so it is not identity; fusion and imagined beings that divide show it comes in degrees. Q-memory and q-intention describe the relations without presupposing identity. Continuity (transitive) is distinguished from connectedness (degree), and successive selves are proposed in place of one underlying person. The principle of self-interest then has no independent force. §I is argued; the priority of connectedness is asserted; §VI is a sketch. | Read |
| [NOTE-379](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-379.md) | Taylor — Consciousness interpreted | A charitable reconstruction, not a defence. Consciousness is a real pattern (Dennett 1991b) in the brain's content-bearing states, and a user-illusion, a pattern so coarse that describing the brain through it is unwitting metaphor. This makes six claims consistent: multiple drafts, global workspace, qualia eliminativism, user-illusion, fallibility and dictatorial authority. The Orwell/Stalin indeterminacy is rebuilt without verificationism: the two readings compress and predict equally, so neither better describes the pattern. | Read |
| [NOTE-380](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-380.md) | Adams & Aizawa — Defending the Bounds of Cognition | A reply to a manuscript of Clark's "Memento's Revenge". It names the coupling-constitution fallacy, coupled is not part of, and restates the 2001 marks of the cognitive. Against Clark's Euler-circle case it separates how a diagram gets its meaning from how a mental image of it does. Against a "brain-tool science" it argues that tools are an open-ended motley. The fallacy is shown by one analogy, and the processing differences are asserted, not measured. | Read |
| [NOTE-381](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-381.md) | Schwitzgebel — Is the United States Phenomenally Conscious? Reply to Kammerer | Three concerns about Kammerer's Sophisticated Anti-Nesting Principle. It may not exclude actual groups, even the US, because citizens may not consciously represent the concrete entity at issue. Condition A's "required" makes group consciousness depend on members' motives: read loosely it gives flickering consciousness with no group-level change, read strictly it can never be met. The Occam-style justification either generalises into causal exclusion or is ad hoc, and group-level explanation is often simpler. Kammerer's is still the best anti-nesting principle the author has met. | Read |
| [NOTE-382](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-382.md) | Shanahan — Simulacra as Conscious Exotica | A Wittgensteinian treatment: consciousness talk is anchored in what is public, so an exotic entity is a candidate only if we could engineer an encounter with it in a shared world. Disembodied chat agents are not candidates; virtually embodied ones could be. On the self: no "self worth preserving" without body, history and autobiographical memory, and no "person behind the mask" for a role player, whose identity is a shifting distribution of roles. Argued by method and scenario; nothing is measured. | Read |
| [NOTE-383](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-383.md) | Against Narrativity | Rejects the descriptive and the normative Narrativity theses. Separates Diachronic from Episodic self-experience, and form-finding, story-telling and revision within Narrativity. Places Dennett, Schechtman, Sartre and himself on that grid. Argues that Episodic lives are normal and can be good, that a from-the-inside memory need not present as one's own, and that self-narration risks falsification. The argument is testimony and distinction-drawing; the empirical claims are asserted. | Read |
| [NOTE-384](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-384.md) | Frankish — Illusionism as a Theory of Consciousness | Illusionism named and defended as a research programme. Phenomenal properties are not instantiated; the task is the illusion problem, to explain why experiences seem to have them. "Quasi-phenomenal" properties, the physical properties introspection misrepresents as phenomenal, separate strong illusionism from a weak illusionism that is argued to be unstable. The case is conservatism, the threat of epiphenomenalism, covert illusionism in reductive theories, a debunking argument and an abductive one from anomalousness. Objections are answered by agreeing to weakened versions of their intuitions. | Read |
| [NOTE-385](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-385.md) | Of Identity and Diversity | Identity is relative to the idea the name stands for: same mass, same life (oak, horse, man), same consciousness (person). A person is a thinking being that "can consider itself as itself", and personal identity reaches as far back as consciousness can be extended, independent of substance. Man and person come apart (prince and cobbler; day-man and night-man), and "person" is "a forensic term" grounding reward and punishment. Argued from cases and intuitions; "the same consciousness" is never defined. | Read |
| [NOTE-386](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-386.md) | Of Personal Identity | No impression of a self is constant, so there is no idea of one; introspection finds only perceptions, and the mind is "a bundle or collection of different perceptions". Its ascribed identity is a fiction of the imagination, like that of ships, churches and plants, produced by resemblance and causation, with memory discovering rather than producing it; hard cases are "grammatical". The Appendix confesses that two principles, the distinctness of perceptions and the imperceptibility of real connexions, leave the unity of the mind unexplained. | Read |
| [NOTE-387](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-387.md) | The Self and the Future | A body-exchange experiment told twice. Third-personally, with selfish choices before and reactions after, it supports "changing bodies". First-personally, as torture preceded by amnesia, character change and implanted memories, fear remains rational at every step, and the step that completes the exchange alters only what happens to someone else. Conceptual undecidability and conventional decision leave the subject unable to expect anything. A "rather shaky" lean toward the bodily answer, as a risk. | Read |
| [NOTE-388](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-388.md) | Social ontology: Some basic principles | Searle restates the 1995 theory in twenty pages. Social facts are facts involving collective intentionality. Institutional facts are the subclass in which collective acceptance imposes a status function, "X counts as Y in C", carrying deontic powers that give desire-independent reasons for action. Language is constitutive, since such facts exist only as represented. He revises the book in two places: status indicators matter in their own right, and some Y terms are free-standing, with no X, as with corporations and money without currency. "We accept (S has power (S does A))", a formula from later in the book, is now ranked as the more general form. Collective intentionality is assumed, not argued for. | Read |
| [NOTE-389](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-389.md) | Ritchie — Social Structures and the Ontology of Social Groups | A structuralist framework for social groups. A social structure is a network of relations constitutively dependent on social factors; it can be overt or covert, intentionally or unintentionally created and instantiated, internal or external to what realizes it. Organized social groups are structured wholes whose internal social structures people occupy; feature social groups are social kinds, nodes in external social structures, which can be intersectional. A restriction to structures that intuitively exist or figure in explanations keeps arbitrary collections out. Motivated, not proved; nothing on agency or minds. | Read |
| [NOTE-390](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-390.md) | Ritchie — What are groups? | Six criteria for any theory of groups (members vary across times and worlds; groups are temporary, contingent and located; coincident groups of one kind can be distinct) rule out groups as non-singular pluralities, fusions, Burge aggregates and Effingham's sets. Proposed: a group is a realization of a structure whose nodes are defined by functional relations; it exists while every node is occupied, members are the occupiers, and two groups are identical at a time only with the same members in the same relations. A sketch of a view, silent on agency. | Read |
| [NOTE-391](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-391.md) | Durkheim — Qu'est-ce qu'un fait social ? | Defines the social fact by exteriority to individual consciousness and external constraint, shown by sanction or by resistance, with diffusion plus independent existence as an equivalent test. Social facts are neither organic nor individual-psychic, so their substrate is society; they are general because collective, and "in each part because in the whole". Statistics isolates collective currents from individual cases. Ways of being are consolidated ways of doing. All of it is definition and illustration; the elimination that yields "a new species" is assumed exhaustive. | Read |
| [NOTE-392](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-392.md) | North — Institutions | An essay, not a proof. Institutions are humanly devised constraints, formal and informal; with technology they fix transaction and production costs and so the incentives of an economy. Village, bazaar and caravan exchange stayed put; early modern European trade evolved, through merchant law, negotiable bills, insurance and a constrained state. Change is path dependent through increasing returns. The game-theoretic motive is one paragraph, the history is narrative, and the one number (the transaction sector's share of GNP) is cited, not derived. | Read |
| [NOTE-393](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-393.md) | Epstein — Social Ontology (SEP) | A survey organised by its author's own distinction: what grounds a social fact versus what anchors the category it falls under. It reports that ontological individualism is widely assumed and now contested; that grounding has displaced supervenience; that convention, collective acceptance, patterns, practices and language are rival theories of anchoring; and that whether anchoring is a distinct relation is disputed. It covers groups, group agency, institutions, race and gender, artifacts, money, law and language. Survey claims are reported, not argued; one citation (Epstein 2016) is missing from the bibliography, and Schwitzgebel 2015 is listed with critics of group phenomenal states. | Read |
| [NOTE-394](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-394.md) | Haslanger — Gender and Race: (What) Are They? | An explicitly ameliorative ("analytical") account. Gender and race are defined as hierarchical social positions: a woman is someone regularly observed or imagined to have bodily features presumed to indicate a female's reproductive role, marked by that within the dominant ideology for subordinate positions, and subordinated in part because of it. Racialized groups are defined in parallel from features presumed to show ancestral links to a region. Gender in general and ethnicity are the non-hierarchical genus. The commonality and normativity objections are met by appeal to the project's values. Stipulative, with no evidence. | Read |
| [NOTE-395](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-395.md) | Hindriks & Guala — Institutions, rules, and equilibria | Institutions are correlated equilibria of coordination games with several equilibria, represented by the players as rules. Searle's constitutive rules are derived from a regulative rule by coining a term that splits it into a base rule and a status rule; the term is conservative and eliminable, so constitutive rules add a name and cognitive economy, not a new kind of fact. The derivation is shown for one toy institution and asserted in general. The reply adds that normativity needs no constitutive rules, because a right is a bundle of other people's regulative obligations. | Read |
| [NOTE-396](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-396.md) | Graham et al. 2013, Moral Foundations Theory | MFT in four claims: a first draft of the moral mind organized in advance of experience, edited by culture, working through fast intuitions, with several foundations because there were several adaptive challenges. Five foundations, each with an evolutionary model (Care: kin selection; Fairness: reciprocal altruism; Loyalty: coalitions; Authority: rank; Sanctity: disease avoidance). MFQ findings: liberals rely on Care and Fairness, conservatives on all five, in every region sampled; Sanctity predicts culture-war attitudes best. Five criteria for foundationhood; Liberty, Efficiency, Ownership and Honesty named as candidates. | Read |
| [NOTE-397](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-397.md) | Yilmaz, Harma & Doğruyol 2021, the MAC-Q in Turkey | An independent validation of the MAC-Q. In Turkish undergraduates (n = 445) seven factors fit the Relevance items (CFI .949, RMSEA .053, SRMR .046) but not the Judgement items (CFI .828) or the full form (CFI .752), as in the original. Family and deference track resistance to change; deference tracks, and fairness counters, opposition to equality. Resource-scarcity primes in Turkey (n = 576) and in a preregistered US MTurk study (n = 921) had no effect. Relevance scores correlate about .1–.3 with prosocial intentions. The seven are strongly intercorrelated. | Read |
| [NOTE-398](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-398.md) | Atari et al. 2023, the MFQ-2 | A new Moral Foundations Questionnaire, built across 25 populations in local languages: 116 candidate items cut to 36, six each for Care, Equality, Proportionality, Loyalty, Authority and Purity, in a "describes me" format. Six factors fit across 19 nations (ESEM CFI .979); reliabilities ω .73–.95; Purity alone fails the alignment invariance threshold. Equality and Proportionality are nearly orthogonal in WEIRD samples. Purity rises most with cultural distance from the U.S. The individualizing–binding split appears in only three national networks. The MFQ-2 predicts criterion scales better than the MFQ-1. | Read |
| [NOTE-399](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-399.md) | Curry et al. 2021, moral molecules | The seven morality-as-cooperation domains are cast as elements whose pairwise combinations are moral "molecules". For each of 21 pairs the authors hypothesise a concept and find a definition or quotation that fits: fraternity, blood revenge, family pride, filial piety, gavelkind, primogeniture, friendship, patriotism, tribute, diplomacy, common ownership, honour, confession, turn-taking, restitution, modesty, mercy, munificence, arbitration, mendicance and queuing. The counts are right (127 positive combinations, 2,186 of mixed valence). The test is a search for confirming instances with no stated protocol, and the theory says it does not predict which molecules exist. | Read |
| [NOTE-400](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-400.md) | Haidt & Joseph 2004, intuitive ethics | An essay, not a study. Humans are innately prepared to feel quick approval or disapproval at four patterns, suffering, hierarchy, reciprocity and purity, each a module with an evolved proper domain and a culturally widened actual domain; ingroup is the best fifth candidate (fn. 15). Three of the four were the concerns all five surveyed works shared; purity was added from the authors' own research on disgust. Cultures vary in actual domains, in which modules they lean on, in what a virtue means, and in how virtues cluster. No data are reported. | Read |
| [NOTE-401](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-401.md) | The Genetical Evolution of Social Behaviour. I | A one-locus, non-overlapping-generation model in which genotypes affect relatives' fitness. Splitting each effect into a part on genes identical by descent (weight r) and a "diluting" part on other genes shows that the former alone fixes the direction of selection, and defines inclusive fitness. Mean inclusive fitness provably increases when no genotype harms others; with harm it only points uphill. A costly act spreads iff −k > 1/r: twice the cost to a sib, eight times to a cousin. | Read |
| [NOTE-402](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-402.md) | The Evolution of Reciprocal Altruism | A verbal model: altruists who withhold help from non-reciprocators beat non-altruists when benefit exceeds cost, encounters are many, partners stable and exposure symmetric. Cleaner-fish hosts that do not eat their cleaners fit it; bird alarm calls are explained by a different, self-interested model. Human friendship, moralistic aggression, gratitude, sympathy, guilt, trust, suspicion and their sham forms are proposed as adaptations regulating exchange, supported by 1960s social psychology, not tested. | Read |
| [NOTE-403](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-403.md) | Haidt 2001, the social intuitionist model | The social intuitionist model: moral judgments appear in consciousness as quick intuitions with an affective valence, and moral reasoning is usually a post-hoc search for supporting reasons, used to persuade others. Six links are drawn, four of them reasoning links, two of them private. The case rests on dual-process research, motivated reasoning, post-hoc confabulation and the weak tie between moral reasoning and moral action, mostly from outside morality. Harmless taboo violations are condemned regardless, and affect predicts the condemnation better than perceived harm. | Read |
| [NOTE-404](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-404.md) | The Logic of Animal Conflict | Five strategies for contests with dangerous weapons, simulated 2,000 times per pairing with payoffs +60 win, −100 serious injury, −2 per scratch, up to +20 for brevity. Hawk is not an ESS; Retaliator is called one though Mouse ties it, so by the paper's own second condition it is only neutrally stable. In a war of attrition no pure strategy is stable; the exponential mixture p(x) = (1/v)e^(−x/v) is. Individual selection can explain limited war. No Hawk–Dove game, no ownership. | Read |
| [NOTE-405](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-405.md) | Rai & Fiske 2011, relationship regulation | A theoretical review. Moral psychology is the regulation of social relationships: an act is right or wrong according to the relational model participants and observers apply, not according to its harm, fairness or purity in the abstract. Fiske's four models (communal sharing, authority ranking, equality matching, market pricing) each carry a moral motive: Unity, Hierarchy, Equality, Proportionality. Honour killing, obedient massacre, blood feud and collateral damage are each read as one motive at work. Purity and ingroup loyalty are both Unity. No new data are reported. | Read |
| [NOTE-406](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-406.md) | Alfano, Cheong & Curry 2024, moral universals in 256 societies | The Morality-as-Cooperation Dictionary (fourteen virtue and vice sub-dictionaries, built by brainstorming, colleague crowd-sourcing and WordNet expansion, culled by two coders at κ ≈ .65–.70) is run with LIWC over 9,653 eHRAF Ethics paragraphs from 256 societies. Most domains are detected in most societies. Region and subsistence matter significantly but slightly. Against the sixty-society hand codes, agreement is about 80% but κ only .08–.25, with false positives outnumbering true ones. The method cannot detect valence. A spot-check found admired theft in four societies and societies that do not value bravery or reciprocity. | Read |
| [NOTE-407](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-407.md) | Baumard, André & Sperber 2013, mutualistic morality | Partner choice with outside options constrains how the gains of cooperation are split: nobody accepts less than they could get elsewhere, so with equal options the stable split is proportional to each partner's contribution of effort and talent. Being chosen then rewards a genuine sense of fairness over calculated fairness. Dictator, ultimatum, trust and public-goods results are read as allocations by perceived entitlement. Twenty-eight commentators accept partner choice and dispute that fairness is all of morality; the authors reply with a broad and a narrow position they have not settled between them. | Read |
| [NOTE-408](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-408.md) | Curry 2016, morality as cooperation: a problem-centred approach | The founding statement of morality-as-cooperation. Nonzero-sum game theory gives seven cooperation problems (kin, coordination, exchange, hawk and dove contest displays, division, possession), and their solutions are said to be what morality is. Each domain gets an animal case, a human case and quotations from moral philosophers. No data are reported. The sharpest content is the predictions on pp. 39–40: non-cooperative behaviour is morally neutral, no culture moralizes "aesthetics or nutrition", and religious morality and food taboos will turn out cooperative. | Read |
| [NOTE-409](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-409.md) | Five Rules for the Evolution of Cooperation | A four-page review. Five mechanisms, each with a b/c threshold derived from a 2×2 game: kin selection r > c/b, direct reciprocity w > c/b, indirect reciprocity q > c/b, network reciprocity b/c > k, group selection b/c > 1 + n/m. For kin, network and group mechanisms the same condition makes cooperators ESS, risk-dominant and advantageous; for the reciprocities RD and AD need more. Derivations are in the unread supplement; the review itself proves nothing. | Read |
| [NOTE-410](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-410.md) | The Genetical Evolution of Social Behaviour. II | Generalizes Part I into two principles (value neighbours by relatedness; discriminate so relatedness is determinate) and applies them qualitatively. Haplodiploid sisters (r = 3/4) favour worker altruism, weakened by multiple mating (eight drones give about 0.31); sibs fight to the death only for a gain above 50%; clonal colonies cooperate and sexual aggregates do not fuse. Evidence is anecdotal, partly contrary (Australian halictines), and anomalies are put down to error or misinterpretation. | Read |
| [NOTE-411](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-411.md) | The Evolution of Cooperation | TIT FOR TAT won Axelrod's two tournaments (14 entries plus random, 200 moves; 62 entries, expected 200 moves) and went to fixation in an ecological replay. Against it, only ALL D or alternation need be checked, so no strategy does better iff w ≥ (T−R)/(T−P) and w ≥ (T−R)/(R−S). ALL D is stable for all w; kinship or a cluster with enough in-cluster interactions lets reciprocity in, and a nice stable strategy resists clusters. ALL C ties with TIT FOR TAT, so stability is neutral, not strict. | Read |
| [NOTE-412](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-412.md) | Curry, Jones Chesters & Van Lissa 2019, the MAC-Q | A 42-item questionnaire for morality-as-cooperation's seven domains, written by the authors from the theory and reduced to three items per subscale. In UK (n = 1,392) and US (n = 469) online samples, seven factors fit the Relevance items well (CFI .98) and the Judgement items poorly (CFI .87). The two sets cannot be pooled into seven domains (CFI .73); the best model keeps fourteen correlated factors. The MFQ's five factors are not recovered. MFQ purity items are rated about as morally relevant as its loyalty and authority items, which the authors concede contradicts the theory. The data cannot be shared. | Read |
| [NOTE-413](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-413.md) | Entropy production fluctuation theorem and the nonequilibrium work relation for free energy differences | A finite-time fluctuation theorem for stochastic dynamics. If the dynamics are Markovian and microscopically reversible (path over reversed path = e^{−βQ}) and the entropy production ω = ln ρ(x_{−τ}) − ln ρ(x_{+τ}) − βQ is odd under time reversal, then P_F(+ω)/P_R(−ω) = e^{ω}. For processes that start and end in equilibrium ω = β(W − ΔF), so the Jarzynski equality is one line. For time-symmetric steady states, P(+ω)/P(−ω) = e^{ω} for any whole number of cycles. At long times it gives a heat fluctuation theorem and Gaussians with variance twice the mean. | Read |
| [NOTE-414](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-414.md) | Belousov-Zhabotinsky reaction | An authoritative short review. BZ: bromate oxidizes an organic reductant, catalysed by Ce, Fe(phen)₃ or other metal ions. Oscillation is a relaxation cycle: autocatalytic HBrO₂ oxidizes the catalyst, Br⁻ (made when Ce⁴⁺ oxidizes bromomalonic acid) shuts the autocatalysis off. Phase resetting by Br⁻, Ag⁺ and Ce⁴⁺ confirms it. Closed: up to several thousand cycles. CSTR: indefinite oscillation, bursting, chaos. Unstirred: trigger waves, targets, spirals, scrolls; in microemulsion, Turing structures. The Oregonator is qualitative only. | Read |
| [NOTE-415](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-415.md) | Life as a manifestation of the second law of thermodynamics | Proposes a "restated second law" (systems pushed from equilibrium resist the applied gradients by every available means) and reads Bénard cells, vortices, life and ecosystem succession as successive gradient destroyers, with the second law "necessary but not sufficient" for life. The law is asserted, not derived. The physics examples show organized structures raising dissipation, not that they must. The ecological evidence is one unreplicated marsh comparison and thermal-scanner and satellite correlations, one of whose tables is internally inconsistent. | Read |
| [NOTE-416](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-416.md) | Statistical Physics of Adaptation | A macrostate fluctuation relation for driven systems, rearranged as an internal-entropy term, a reversal term and Ψ − Φ = −ln⟨e^{−βΔQ}⟩. It is read as a tendency of driven matter towards states formed by reliable work absorption and dissipation, "adaptation" without replication. The relation is exact. The tendency needs the other two terms held fixed, which the paper arranges only in contrived one-particle landscapes; the many-body claim and the "adaptive resonance" simulation are argued and reported, not shown. | Read |
| [NOTE-417](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-417.md) | Nonequilibrium Equality for Free Energy Differences | The nonequilibrium work relation. Switch a classical system that starts in canonical equilibrium from H_0 to H_1 at any finite rate. Then ⟨e^{−βW}⟩ = e^{−βΔF} exactly, whatever the path or the switching time (Eq. 2). It is proved for an isolated Hamiltonian system by Liouville's theorem, for a reservoir under weak coupling, and for a Nosé–Hoover thermostat with no extra assumption. Thermodynamic integration and the Zwanzig formula are its limits, ⟨W⟩ ≥ ΔF follows by Jensen, and Gaussian work gives W_diss = βσ²/2. Its practical reach is work fluctuations of order k_BT. | Read |
| [NOTE-418](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-418.md) | Pattern formation outside of equilibrium | Read for the record's question only. Patterns away from equilibrium are classified by the linear instability of a uniform state (type I_s, III_o, I_o) and described near onset by universal amplitude equations. There is in general no free energy or Lyapunov functional selecting them. Bénard's own 1900 hexagons were surface-tension-driven, not Rayleigh's buoyancy convection. Closed chemical systems must reach equilibrium, so their patterns are transients or need pumping. No global minimization principle exists beyond a perturbative one near threshold. | Skimmed |
| [NOTE-419](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-419.md) | Statistical physics of self-replication | A coarse-grained second law, β⟨ΔQ⟩ + ln π(II→I) + ΔS_int ≥ 0, applied to self-replication: the heat a replicator releases per copy is bounded below by the log-odds against its copy falling apart within one generation, so durable, fast copies cost more heat. The general bound is sound. The E. coli and RNA numbers rest on an upper bound on the reverse probability that is argued from biology, not derived, and one printed logarithm has its argument inverted. | Read |
| [NOTE-420](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-420.md) | Stochastic thermodynamics, fluctuation theorems and molecular machines | The 2012 reference review of stochastic thermodynamics. For Markovian dynamics coupled to baths of fixed temperature, work, heat and a stochastic entropy s = −ln p(x(τ),τ) are defined on single trajectories. Every known integral, detailed and Crooks-type fluctuation theorem follows from one master theorem, as the log-ratio of a path's weight under the original and a conjugate dynamics. Dissipation measures how distinguishable forward and reverse paths are. Feedback adds the mutual information I to the second law (Sagawa–Ueda). Machine efficiency and efficiency at maximum power follow from a cycle decomposition. The integral theorems are not proofs of the second law, because the dynamics build irreversibility in. | Read |
| [NOTE-421](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-421.md) | Life as we know it | The flow of internal and active states in an ergodic system with a Markov blanket, written in Helmholtz form, can be redescribed as gradient descent on a variational free energy once the variational density is set equal to the posterior over external states. On that redescription such systems "appear" to infer and to bound their own entropy, which the paper calls the marks of life. One simulated soup shows a blanket-enclosed cluster that predicts external motion and falls apart under lesions. The free energy is variational, not thermodynamic. The candle flame is excluded by a one-sentence assertion. The paper's own reported p-value is not reproduced by the null it describes. | Read |
| [NOTE-422](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-422.md) | Comments on a derivation and application of the ‘maximum entropy production’ principle | Two corrections to Dewar. First, Dewar 2005 derives MaxEP far from equilibrium from a fluctuation theorem plus a quadratic approximation to the flux distribution, used as if valid for all f. Both can hold together only for small F, where A(F) = ∂λ/∂F is constant: the linear regime. So the orthogonality condition ∂D/∂F = 4λ, which yields both maximum and minimum dissipation, fails for nonlinear constitutive relations, as an exactly solvable spin chain shows. Second, Dewar 2003's SOC divergence disappears once the quartic term is kept. | Read |
| [NOTE-423](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-423.md) | Nonequilibrium thermodynamics and maximum entropy production in the Earth system | A review by MEP's leading Earth-system advocate. MEP: given enough degrees of freedom, a system adopts the steady state of maximum entropy production. It is distinguished from Prigogine's minimum principle, which describes relaxation toward a steady state and does not select among several. Two-box models show MEP arising from a flux–force trade-off. The Earth's entropy budget is estimated and dominated by absorption of sunlight. MEP fits are surveyed from Paltridge's climate model to sediment ecology, and life is argued to push the planet toward MEP. The author concedes MEP is not established. | Read |
| [NOTE-424](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-424.md) v2 | Time, Structure and Fluctuations | Prigogine's Nobel statement. Near equilibrium δ²S < 0 with ½∂δ²S/∂t = P > 0 makes δ²S a Lyapounov function, and in the strictly linear regime (constant L_ρρ′) the steady state minimizes entropy production. Far from equilibrium ½∂δ²S/∂t = ΣδJ_ρδX_ρ has no fixed sign, and ΣδJδX ≥ 0 is offered only as a sufficient stability condition. The Brusselator goes unstable at B > 1 + A². Dissipative structures are instabilities of the thermodynamic branch, triggered by fluctuations and sustained by exchange with the outside.
