The microscopic §§6–7 is a programme, linked to macroscopic thermodynamics only "in the linear region". | Read |
| [NOTE-425](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-425.md) | Energy flow and the organization of life | An essay arguing that life, beginning with the reductive citric-acid cycle, was forced into existence as a channel relieving geochemical free-energy stresses, like lightning or a hurricane. The framework is Jaynesian entropy maximization over states that include currents, not entropy-production principles. The emergence of life is a phase-transition "collapse" of a metastable abiotic earth. The structure of core metabolism is the only evidence. The authors state that the thesis is not quantitatively demonstrated. | Read |
| [NOTE-426](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-426.md) | Information theory explanation of the fluctuation theorem, maximum entropy production and self-organized criticality in non-equilibrium stationary states | Jaynes's maximum-entropy inference, applied to microscopic paths of an open stationary system, gives p_Γ = exp(A_Γ)/Z. The action is a symmetric end-point term plus τσ_Γ/2k_B. A fluctuation theorem p(σ) = e^{τσ/k_B} p(−σ) and ⟨σ⟩ ≥ 0 follow exactly. Maximum entropy production follows only in a mean-field approximation, and only if the number of paths increases with their irreversible action, which is assumed. The self-organized-criticality corollary is a mean-field divergence. As Grinstein and Linsker showed, it does not survive keeping the quartic term. | Read |
| [NOTE-427](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-427.md) | The Chemical Basis of Morphogenesis | Turing's reaction–diffusion theory of pattern. Linearize about a homogeneous steady state of M morphogens on a ring of N cells: each Fourier mode grows or decays independently, and the fastest-growing one sets the pattern. Six forms of onset are possible. For two morphogens, stationary waves of finite wave-length need bc < 0 and 4√(μ′ν′)/(μ′+ν′) < (d−a)/√(−bc) < (μ′+ν′)/√(μ′ν′) (9.4a), with instability if d√(μ′/ν′)/√(−bc) − a√(ν′/μ′)/√(−bc) > 2 (9.4b). These cannot both hold when μ′ = ν′. A computed 20-cell ring gives three lobes. | Read |
| [NOTE-428](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-428.md) | How effective psychological treatments work: mechanisms of change in cognitive behavioural therapy and beyond | A history-and-argument paper. It separates common factors from mechanisms of change, holds that a treatment's success does not validate its theory, and proposes one mechanism for all effective therapy: the person, "stuck" in an exaggerated negative meaning (Theory A) maintained by their own reactions, builds and tests a less threatening alternative (Theory B), a meaning-making flexibility. The proposal is argued from clinical examples and cited trials. The paper itself says mechanism research has not yet met Kazdin's standards. | Read |
| [NOTE-429](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-429.md) | The Emerging Field of Emotion Regulation: An Integrative Review | Gross's founding review. Emotions are modulable response tendencies. Regulation, defined as influencing which emotions one has, when, and how they are experienced and expressed, is split from coping, mood regulation and defense, and sorted into five families by where it acts in emotion generation: situation selection, situation modification, attentional deployment, cognitive change, response modulation. The paper's own open problem is whether regulation can be told apart from generation at all. | Read |
| [NOTE-430](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-430.md) | Relational Frame Theory: Some Implications for Understanding and Treating Human Psychopathology | RFT stated for clinicians. Human relating is bidirectional and arbitrarily applicable, a generalized operant under contextual control, with mutual and combinatorial entailment and transformation of function. Relating relations explains metaphor, rules are networks whose coordination with events regulates behaviour, and the self is content, process and context. These are offered as the reason direct-contingency behaviour therapy is "inadequate", and ACT's acceptance, defusion and values are given RFT readings explicitly labelled heuristic. | Read |
| [NOTE-431](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-431.md) | Acceptance and Commitment Therapy: Model, Processes and Outcomes | The ACT model, read in an early manuscript. Psychopathology is psychological inflexibility: cognitive fusion (fed by literality, reason-giving and emotional control) supports experiential avoidance, and both displace present-moment contact and values. Six processes restore flexibility. The manuscript's evidence: AAQ correlations with distress (21 studies), component studies of acceptance and defusion, mediation in seven trials, and weighted outcome effect sizes (d = .49 post and .69 follow-up against active treatments). | Read |
| [NOTE-432](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-432.md) | Explanatory Models for Psychiatric Illness | Psychiatric etiology is multilevel, non-aggregative and full of causal loops, so law-based explanation fails and so do both hard reduction and hard emergence. Mechanistic explanation proceeds by local decomposition and difficult reassembly. Following Marr, biology implements the functions psychology describes and does not replace them. The argument is philosophical, illustrated by an alcohol-dependence sketch and cited findings; it reports no data of its own. | Read |
| [NOTE-433](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-433.md) | Impulsive action: emotional impulses and their control | Frijda's action-readiness theory applied to impulsive action. Automatic appraisal of concern pertinence elicits a state of action readiness, a motive to change a self–object relation, with control precedence. Emotions differ by mode of action readiness. Events draw several appraisals at once, and the resulting states of readiness interact, so impulse control and much emotion regulation are themselves emotional. Purposive impulsive action is grounded neurally in anticipating an action's sensory effects (PPC, premotor, cerebellum), with frontostriatal circuits for stopping. | Read |
| [NOTE-434](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-434.md) | The theory of constructed emotion: an active inference account of interoception and categorization | Barrett's theory of constructed emotion, stated through predictive coding. The brain runs an allostatic internal model. Its predictions are concepts, initiated in agranular limbic cortex and corrected by prediction error. Interoceptive consequences are felt as affect. An emotion is a categorization by an emotion concept. Degeneracy and population thinking deny emotion categories neural essences. Basic-emotion and causal appraisal theories are rejected; descriptive appraisals are kept as products of categorization. | Read |
| [NOTE-435](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-435.md) | A Network Theory of Mental Disorders | Borsboom states the network theory as five principles: complexity, symptom-component correspondence, direct causal connections between symptoms, disorders as tightly coupled symptom clusters, and hysteresis. Disorder is an alternative stable state of a strongly connected network that outlasts its trigger; health is the stable state of a weakly connected one. Treatment is symptom, external-field or network intervention. The evidence offered is indirect, and the theory is presented as an organizing framework, not a fitted model. | Read |
| [NOTE-436](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-436.md) | Psychological Flexibility as a Fundamental Aspect of Health | A narrative review that defines psychological flexibility by four dynamic abilities (adapting to situations, reconfiguring resources, shifting perspective, balancing domains). Flexible regulation beats any fixed strategy (Bonanno 2004; Cheng 2001). Inflexibility marks depression and anxiety, and cardiac vagal control may integrate its forms. Causal direction is open. It recommends experience sampling and multi-informant measures over global self-report. | Read |
| [NOTE-437](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-437.md) | The Future of Intervention Science: Process-Based Therapy | A programme paper. Thirty years of DSM-linked trials never turned syndromes into diseases, so evidence-based therapy should target theory-derived, empirically supported processes of change with procedures shown to move them. Its distinctive commitments are a falsification rule for models of change, moderated mediation as a basis for a new nosology, and an idiographic, functional-analytic diagnosis. It offers predictions and cited examples, not new data. | Read |
| [NOTE-438](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-438.md) | A meta-analytic review of experiments examining the effects of extrinsic rewards on intrinsic motivation | 128 experiments on interesting tasks, separate meta-analyses for free-choice behaviour and self-reported interest. Tangible rewards expected for engaging in, completing or doing well at a task undermine free-choice intrinsic motivation (d from −0.28 to −0.40), and less so self-reports; unexpected and task-noncontingent rewards do not; positive feedback enhances, except for children's free choice. The pattern fits cognitive evaluation theory and contradicts Eisenberger and Cameron's helplessness and learned-industriousness account. | Read |
| [NOTE-439](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-439.md) | The "What" and "Why" of Goal Pursuits: Human Needs and the Self-Determination of Behavior | Needs in SDT are innate psychological nutriments, identified functionally by the costs of their thwarting, not by individual differences in their strength. The three needs organize findings on intrinsic motivation and internalization, predict that both the "why" (autonomous vs controlled regulation) and the "what" (intrinsic vs extrinsic aspirations) of goals affect well-being, and explain need substitutes and rigid defences when needs are thwarted. Autonomy is self-organization, defended as an evolved, domain-general design feature. | Read |
| [NOTE-440](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-440.md) | Basic psychological need theory: Advancements, critical themes, and future directions | The authors set out nine criteria a basic psychological need must meet, separate need frustration from mere lack of satisfaction, review need frustration's costs (ill-being, compensatory behaviour, need substitutes) and propose it as transdiagnostic, review the interplay with sleep, food, sex and safety, map need-supportive and need-thwarting practices on a circumplex, and argue that the needs are universal in their effects ("output side") while the practices that satisfy them vary ("input side"). Novelty, morality and beneficence are weighed as fourth needs and not yet admitted. | Read |
| [NOTE-441](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-441.md) | Self-Regulation and the Problem of Human Autonomy: Does Psychology Need Choice, Self-Determination, and Will? | SDT's autonomy is "regulation by the self": acting with reflective endorsement, graded along the internalization continuum, compatible with external influence, dependence and a single option. Ryan and Deci align it with phenomenological and hierarchical (Frankfurt, Dworkin) accounts and use that definition to answer behaviourist, social-cognitive, reductionist, nonconscious-priming, choice-overload and cultural critiques. Autonomy lies not in being an uncaused cause but in the capacity to "reflectively endorse or reject prompted actions" (p. 1573). | Read |
| [NOTE-442](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-442.md) | Self-determination theory and the facilitation of intrinsic motivation, social development, and well-being | SDT's canonical short statement. Social contexts that support competence, autonomy and relatedness sustain intrinsic motivation and let people internalize and integrate extrinsic regulations; controlling, rejecting or overchallenging contexts undermine intrinsic motivation and leave regulations external or introjected. Autonomy is graded: four kinds of extrinsic motivation lie on a continuum of relative autonomy (Ryan & Connell's quasi-simplex). Basic needs are defined by their consequences for well-being, and need-deprivation is offered as a principal source of distress and psychopathology. | Read |
| [NOTE-443](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-443.md) | Précis of Breakdown of Will | Hyperbolic discounting makes preference reverse with delay alone, so a person is a population of interests that forestall one another. Will is the recursive bundling of choices by treating each as a precedent, which gives an intertemporal repeated prisoner's dilemma and makes Kavka's toxin rational to drink. The same recursion predicts legalism, "lapse districts", motivated misperception and compulsive over-control, and premature satiation of appetite limits what will can do for emotional reward. | Read |
| [NOTE-444](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-444.md) | An Organizational Account of Biological Functions | Functions are contributions to a differentiated self-maintaining organization by traits that organization produces and maintains (C1–C3). Closure alone grounds teleology and normativity but is shared by flames and hurricanes; differentiation is what makes functions meaningful. The account unifies "why is T there?" and "what is T for?" in current organization, and distinguishes functional, useful, dysfunctional, primary and secondary contributions. Reproduction is left to future "second-order" self-maintaining systems. | Read |
| [NOTE-445](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-445.md) | The Diffusion Decision Model: Theory and Data for Two-Choice Decision Tasks | The full diffusion model (drift v with across-trial SD η, boundaries 0 and a, starting point z with range s_z, non-decision time Ter with range s_t) fitted by the χ² quantile method. In three dot-motion experiments with 14–17 participants difficulty maps to drift alone, speed–accuracy instructions to boundaries alone, and a 75:25 proportion mainly to starting point; RT distribution shape is invariant across conditions. Drift variability gives slow errors and starting-point variability fast ones. Reviews aging, aphasia and neural-firing applications. | Read |
| [NOTE-446](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-446.md) | The Expected Value of Control: An Integrative Theory of Anterior Cingulate Cortex Function | The paper proposes that dACC estimates the expected value of control: probability-weighted payoff of a control signal minus an intrinsic cost rising with its intensity. dACC uses that estimate to specify the identity and intensity of the control signal, which lPFC and subcortical structures (STN, LC) implement. The account unifies conflict monitoring, reward and prediction-error responses, effort costs and the override of defaults. It is a synthesis without a fitted model, and it leaves open how EVC is computed and what the cost function is. | Read |
| [NOTE-447](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-447.md) | The Emperor's New Markov Blankets | Pearl blankets (conditional-independence shields in a Bayesian network) and Friston blankets (agent–environment boundaries in the world) are different constructs conflated in the free-energy literature. The four-way partition needs a chosen internal node, sensorimotor labels and steady state; in Friston's soup it also needs electrochemical-only coupling and a fixed cluster size. Realist use needs metaphysical premises; instrumental use is innocent but settles nothing about boundaries. | Read |
| [NOTE-448](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-448.md) | Goal Striving, Need Satisfaction, and Longitudinal Well-Being: The Self-Concordance Model | Sheldon and Elliot's integrated model of goal striving. Self-concordance (identified + intrinsic − introjected − external reasons) predicts sustained effort, effort fully mediates attainment (Study 1 β = .02 ns vs .76; Study 3 β = .08 ns vs .72), attainment predicts change in well-being more strongly when goals are self-concordant (interaction β = .11, .23), and accumulated daily autonomy, competence and relatedness partly mediate attainment's effect on well-being (Study 3). Three correlational student samples; SEM fits reached after adding paths the modification indices suggested. | Read |
| [NOTE-449](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-449.md) | The Time Course of Perceptual Choice: The Leaky, Competing Accumulator Model | The LCA: dx_i = [ρ_i − k x_i − β Σ_{j≠i} x_j] dt/τ + ξ_i √(dt/τ), with x_i floored at 0. For two choices x₁ − x₂ is an OU process with K = k − β; d′(t) = d_asy (1 − e^{−Kt})/√(1 − e^{−2Kt}) and d_asy = (2v/σ)√(1/K). On new time–accuracy data the OU curve beats diffusion-with-drift-variance in 7 of 9 fits (combined likelihood ratio about 62). Leak versus inhibition predicts recency versus primacy; 2 of 6 participants favoured the end cluster and 2 opposed it. A fixed-accuracy criterion gives Hick's law in all three models simulated, so it does not discriminate them. | Read |
| [NOTE-450](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-450.md) | The Markov blanket trick: On the scope of the free energy principle and active inference | The FEP is true of things defined its way, but its scope is limited: a Markov blanket fits only some things, is placed where the model needs it, and cannot represent relational properties or a boundary the system makes. Its real job is to give any system the partition variational Bayes requires. Active-inference models smuggle perception and action into hand-built generative models. A conceptual critique; no new formal result and no data. | Read |
| [NOTE-451](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-451.md) | Hierarchically organized behavior and its neural foundations: A reinforcement learning perspective | The paper introduces hierarchical RL (options) to psychology and neuroscience as a response to RL's scaling problem. An actor-critic options model learns faster with useful options (doorways in the rooms task) and slower or worse with unhelpful ones (windows; a shortcut ignored). It proposes DLPFC for option identity, DLS for option-specific policies, and OFC for option-specific values and option-level prediction errors. It predicts phasic dopamine at subtask boundaries and a possible neural pseudo-reward. Option discovery is named as the central problem. | Read |
| [NOTE-452](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-452.md) | The Intimate Contest for Self-Command | Schelling's essay proposes "egonomics", the art of managing oneself. Its core claim is that many self-control tactics are the tactics for managing another person, applied to a "sometime self" who will be in command later. It gives a taxonomy of habits by time profile, onset time, warning time, cost, visibility, damage function and self-aggravation. It refuses a general answer to which self is authentic. It estimates the stakes at "on the order of a trillion dollars" for the current population. | Read |
| [NOTE-453](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-453.md) | Evolution of the Internal Family Systems Model | Schwartz's short statement of the IFS model. A person is "an ecology of relatively discrete minds", parts forced into managerial, exiled or firefighting roles by experience, which can return to valuable roles. At the core is a Self with leadership qualities (the eight Cs), reached when parts "step back", as in the founding case of "Diane". The Self is an active healer, not a witness. A practitioner's essay; its evidence is clinical experience. | Read |
| [NOTE-454](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-454.md) | Sophisticated Inference | Expected free energy evaluated recursively over the beliefs an agent would hold after each plausible outcome turns active inference into a pruned tree search over belief states, formally analogous to Bellman recursion. Small simulations show it planning through short-term costs (an aversive cue; a maze barrier needing horizon four) and exploring by novelty. Its scalability is argued from pruning, not demonstrated; its first-principles grounding is cited, not developed. | Read |
| [NOTE-455](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-455.md) | The Normative Constitution of Agency | Korsgaard asks whether agency is natural (a movement caused by the right mental state) or normatively constituted (a movement backed by an agent whose unity is achieved by following norms). Ordinary attribution of action implies activity and identity. Self-constitution explains both: the categorical and hypothetical imperatives give action the form of self-determined efficacy, and the maxims chosen under them constitute practical identity. Reflective self-consciousness breaks given unity and sets the task of rebuilding it, so unity of agency is "an achievement, not a given". | Read |
| [NOTE-456](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-456.md) | Participatory sense-making: An enactive approach to social cognition | An enactive account of social cognition built on coordination dynamics. When coordination and the encounter shape each other, the interaction gains its own precarious operational closure; it is social only if the interactors stay autonomous. Coordinated sense-making then ranges from one partner's sense-making being modulated to meanings made jointly. Conceptual throughout; evidence is illustrative (corridor, charades, mother–infant timing, perceptual crossing). | Read |
| [NOTE-457](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-457.md) | Internal Family Systems Therapy | Chapter 1 only. Schwartz tells how IFS grew out of structural and strategic family therapy. Clients with bulimia described warring "parts". Coercing parts made matters worse, and curiosity revealed that they protected the client. When interfering parts were asked to "step back", a calm, compassionate "Self" appeared that needed no development. The chapter ends with eight essentials: ecological respect for protectors, Self-leadership, unblending, attention to polarizations, non-pathologizing burdens, movement between system levels, and innate self-healing. | Skimmed |
| [NOTE-458](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-458.md) | Defining Agency: Individuality, Normativity, Asymmetry, and Spatio-temporality in Action | Minimal agency needs a self-individuated system that is the source of modulations of its coupling with the environment and regulates them by norms it generates. An autonomous organization, a precarious network of mutually enabling processes, generates all three when it adaptively modulates its coupling for its own maintenance. Life is sufficient, not necessary. The definition is a proposal: the authors say asymmetry's formalization is incomplete and modeller-relative. | Read |
| [NOTE-459](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-459.md) | Uncertainty-based competition between prefrontal and dorsolateral striatal systems for behavioral control | Habit and goal-directed action are model-free (cached values) and model-based (tree search) reinforcement learners, arbitrated by which controller's Bayesian posterior over action value has the lower variance. Over-training shifts control to the cache because tree search accrues computational noise per step. Task complexity and proximity to reward keep the tree in control. It also predicts that incentive learning should become unnecessary when the caching system is lesioned. | Read |
| [NOTE-460](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-460.md) | Précis: Being No One | Metzinger's own sketch of the self-model theory. Six constraints (globality, presentationality, transparency, convolved holism, dynamicity, perspectivalness) build up consciousness; a transparent self-model yields phenomenal selfhood, so "no such things as selves exist in the world"; transparency is graded, inversely proportional to the attentional availability of earlier processing stages. Phenomenal unity is one global functional cluster. The first-person perspective is a model of the intentionality relation, whose iteration gives introspective and social intelligence. A theory statement: no new evidence. | Read |
| [NOTE-461](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-461.md) | The Physics of Optimal Decision Making: A Formal Analysis of Models of Performance in Two-Alternative Forced-Choice Tasks | Proves the pure DDM is the continuum limit of the SPRT and so the optimal two-choice decider. The linearised Usher–McClelland model decouples into a difference process, an O-U with λ = w − k, and a stable sum process, so it reduces to the DDM when k = w and both are large; feedforward inhibition does so at u = 1, pooled inhibition with fast inhibitory units, the race model never. The reward-rate-optimal threshold is unique and depends only on the total delay, giving a parameter-free optimal performance curve (peak decision time about 0.2 of the delay at ER about 18%). | Read |
| [NOTE-462](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-462.md) | An Accumulator Model for Spontaneous Neural Activity Prior to Self-Initiated Movement | A three-parameter leaky stochastic accumulator, δx = (I − kx)Δt + cξ√Δt, fitted to Libet-task waiting times (k = 0.5, I = 0.11, threshold at the 80th percentile of output), reproduces the readiness potential (r² = 0.96) and the linear mean–SD relation of waiting times (r = 0.9). In a variant with random clicks, fast responders showed a slow negativity before the click (P < 0.005), and fast and slow clicks were spread equally through the trial (P = 0.64). The neural decision to move is placed at the threshold crossing about 150 ms before movement. | Read |
| [NOTE-463](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-463.md) | Model-based and model-free Pavlovian reward learning: Revaluation, revision, and revelation | The paper argues that Pavlovian prediction has a model-based form. A cue that predicted disgusting salt was instantly "wanted" in a novel salt appetite, before retasting, with mesolimbic activation. That needs identity-specific prediction revalued by current state, which no model-free cache provides. It proposes "defocusing" of the predicted outcome as a model-based source of apparent habit. It argues dopamine can carry model-based evaluations. A kappa-transformation model fits the data but is described as phenomenological, and no algorithm is offered. | Read |
| [NOTE-464](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-464.md) | A Planning Agent's Self-Governance over Time | Bratman's model of a planning agent's self-governance over time. It needs (1) planned temporally extended activity with a time-frame to "plan's end", (2) synchronic self-governance along the way, (3) cross-temporal links of intention that are intrapersonal analogues of shared intention, so the agent acts "together" with herself, and (4) "diachronicalized" standpoints holding a conditional, reflexive end of diachronic self-governance. The model makes some willpower and resistance to shuffling self-governed and excludes the toxin case. Argued by cases, not tested. | Read |
| [NOTE-465](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-465.md) | The Sources of Normativity | Korsgaard's Tanner Lectures. The normative question (what justifies morality's claims on us) is not answered by voluntarism, substantive realism or reflective endorsement alone. Reflective consciousness forces us to act on reasons, and so to identify with a law: a "practical identity", the description under which we value ourselves. Obligation is the reflective rejection of a threat to that identity. Valuing any identity commits us to valuing humanity, reasons are public, and animals' pain can obligate us. Argued, but "sketchy" by its own account. | Read |
| [NOTE-466](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-466.md) | HDDM: Hierarchical Bayesian Estimation of the Drift-Diffusion Model in Python | HDDM fits the full DDM (v, a, z, t with sv, st, sz) by MCMC in PyMC, with group-level priors such as μ_a ~ Gamma(1.5, 0.75), μ_v ~ N(2, 3), z_j ~ invlogit(N(μ_z, σ_z²)), and the Navarro–Fuss likelihood. A patsy-style regressor lets a parameter vary with a trial covariate. In simulations with 12 subjects and 20–150 trials, hierarchical Bayes had the lowest trimmed recovery error for every parameter and the highest power to detect drift differences and covariate effects, by up to 20% for the latter with larger effects and few trials. | Read |
| [NOTE-467](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-467.md) | The Haken–Kelso–Bunz (HKB) model: from matter to movement to mind | Kelso's retrospective on HKB. The relative-phase equation dφ/dt = −a sin φ − 2b sin 2φ has stable in-phase and anti-phase states for b/a > 1/4 and only in-phase below; with noise the switch is a nonequilibrium phase transition with critical fluctuations and slowing. Symmetry breaking gives metastability (relative coordination); merged with Kuramoto it models groups. The same dynamics is reported within and between people and in brain activity. A first-person account, not a review. | Read |
| [NOTE-468](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-468.md) | Biological organisation as closure of constraints | A constraint acts on a process while being conserved at that process's time scale; closure is a set of constraints, each directly dependent on and generative for another, that cannot be split. This separates closure from cycles of processes, grounds functions, and gives a boundary criterion, refined by a preliminary count-based tendency to closure for symbionts and by nested separated closures for levels. Applied only to schematic examples; closure is not claimed to define organisms. | Read |
| [NOTE-469](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-469.md) | Thine Own Self: True Self-Concept Accessibility and Meaning in Life | Five studies with students. True-self accessibility, the residual speed of "me" judgments for one's own true-self traits, predicts MLQ presence of meaning (β = .29, .24, .19 in Studies 1, 2, 4) beyond actual-self accessibility, affect, need satisfaction, liking, overlap, state self-esteem and AI-3 authenticity. Subliminal true-self primes raised reported meaning against actual-self primes (Study 3, ANCOVA F = 4.46) and against disliked actual-self primes (Study 5, contrast F = 5.07), with disliked true-self traits as effective as liked ones. | Read |
| [NOTE-470](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-470.md) | “First we invented stories, then they changed us”: The Evolution of Narrative Identity | McAdams's review essay on narrative identity: "the internalized and evolving story a person invents to explain how he or she has become the person he or she is becoming". Storytelling is placed in human evolution as a tool for simulating social life and binding groups. The self is layered as actor, then agent, then author, with the life story arriving in adolescence on the back of autobiographical memory, theory of mind and autobiographical reasoning. Story features predict well-being and generativity. Culture supplies master narratives. Claims are cited, not shown. | Read |
| [NOTE-471](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-471.md) | Bayesian Interpolation | Evidence ≈ best-fit likelihood × Occam factor, the ratio of posterior to prior accessible parameter volume. At the evidence maximum the regulariser's χ² equals γ = Σ λ_a/(λ_a + α), the number of well-determined parameters, and the data χ² equals N − γ. Polynomials show an "Occam hill" in k, and the true Hermite model wins on data drawn from it. | Read |
| [NOTE-472](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-472.md) | Natural Gradient Works Efficiently in Learning | Natural gradient ∇̃L = G⁻¹∇L is steepest descent under metric G (Theorem 1); for a statistical model G is the Fisher information. Online learning w_{t+1} = w_t − (1/t)∇̃l is Fisher efficient, Ṽ_t = G⁻¹/t + O(1/t²) (Theorem 2). Explicit G and G⁻¹ for a perceptron (Theorems 3–4) and the natural gradient ∇L·WᵀW on the matrix group (Theorem 6). The plateau claim for multilayer perceptrons is a conjecture resting on Yang & Amari (1997). | Read |
| [NOTE-473](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-473.md) | A Widely Applicable Bayesian Information Criterion | The Bayes free energy of a singular model is nL_n(w0) + λ log n − (m − 1) log log n + O_p(1), with λ the RLCT. The posterior mean of nL_n(w) at inverse temperature 1/log n (WBIC) reproduces the first two terms without knowing λ, equals BIC for regular models, and two temperatures estimate λ. In reduced-rank regression WBIC chose the true rank 100/100 times, and estimated RLCTs within about 0.5 of theory. | Read |
| [NOTE-474](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-474.md) | Traces of Class/Cross-Class Structure Pervade Deep Learning Spectra | G = Gclass + Gcross + Gwithin + Gc=c′ (Eq. 6.4); knockouts give C outliers to Gclass, a C² mini-bulk to Gcross, the bulk to Gwithin, in log-spectra. Gˡ = Σ w (hhᵀ) ⊗ (δδᵀ) (Eq. 7.8), so gc ≈ hc ⊗ δc. K-FAC Hˡ ⊗ Δˡ misaligns G's outliers; CFAC Ave_c Hˡc ⊗ Δˡc aligns them at deep layers. For multinomial logistic regression on a Gaussian model the expected Fisher has C outliers, C(C−2) mini-bulk and a bulk (Theorem 8.1). | Read |
| [NOTE-475](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-475.md) | Deep Equals Shallow for ReLU Networks in Kernel Regimes | For κ(xᵀx′) on S^(d−1), µ_k = (ω_(d−2)/ω_(d−1)) ∫ κ(t)P_k(t)(1 − t²)^((d−3)/2) dt (Eq. 8), shared by N(d, k) ~ k^(d−2) harmonics. Theorem 1: an endpoint expansion κ(1 − t) = p(t) + c t^ν + o(t^ν) with ν non-integer gives µ_k ~ C k^(−d−2ν+1), parity-dependent. ReLU kernels have ν = 1/2 (NTK) and 3/2 (random features) at any depth, so the deep NTK decays as k^(−d) and the deep RF kernel as k^(−d−2) (Corollaries 2–3). MNIST and Fashion-MNIST accuracy is flat in depth from L = 2 to 5 (Table 1). | Read |
| [NOTE-476](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-476.md) | Prevalence of Neural Collapse during the terminal phase of deep learning training | NC1 Σ_W → 0 (Tr(Σ_WΣ_B†)/C falls), NC2 centred means → simplex ETF (cosines → −1/(C−1)), NC3 W ∝ Ṁᵀ, NC4 nearest class mean; 7 datasets × 3 nets, 480 models. Median TPT gains: +0.35 points test accuracy, +0.0252 DeepFool robustness. NC1–2 ⇒ NC3–4 for Webb–Lowe and max-margin (Theorems 2, 4). Optimal small-noise error exponent C/(C−1)·1/4, only at simplex ETFs (Theorem 5). | Read |
| [NOTE-477](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-477.md) | Bayesian model reduction | The free energy of any model that differs from a fitted one only in its prior is ln E_Q[P̃/P] plus the fitted free energy, with closed forms for Gaussian, Dirichlet, beta, gamma, categorical and multinomial priors. Greedy pruning by this quantity recovers sparse structure in three simulations. Hierarchical models invert as a cascade of reductions. | Read |
| [NOTE-478](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-478.md) | The Full Spectrum of Deepnet Hessians at Scale | Hess = H + G (Gauss–Newton). At 28M parameters the Hessian has a bulk and about C outliers; outliers are in G, the bulk mostly in H; H is symmetric and power-law, φ ∝ \|λ\|⁻²·⁷ (R² 0.99), not Marchenko–Pastur or semicircle. G = A₁ + A₂ + B₁ + B₂ (Eq. 5): C class means give the outliers, cross-class spread and within-pair spread give two bulks. FastLanczos costs O(Mnp) time and O(p) memory. | Read |
| [NOTE-479](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-479.md) | Measurements of Three-Level Hierarchical Structure in the Outliers in the Spectrum of Deepnet Hessians | G = (1/n)ΔΔᵀ with δᵢ,c,c′ = column c′ of diag(√p)(I − 1pᵀ)∂f/∂θ. C² group means δc,c′, C cluster centres δc; G = G₀ + G₁ + G₂ + G₃ (Eq. 28). Top-C eigenvalues of G ≈ those of G₁ = (C−1)Ave_c δcδcᵀ, from a C×C Gram matrix, with λc(G) ≥ λc(G₁₊₂) ≥ λc(G₁). 18,000 CIFAR/MNIST models plus ImageNet VGG16/ResNet50. Group means switch from clustering by logit c′ to by class c during training. | Read |
| [NOTE-480](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-480.md) | Shampoo: Preconditioned Stochastic Tensor Optimization | Matrix case: L = εI + Σ GGᵀ, R = εI + Σ GᵀG, W ← W − ηL^{-1/4}GR^{-1/4}. Lemma 8: εI + (1/r)Σ ggᵀ ⪯ L^{1/2} ⊗ R^{1/2} for rank-r gradients, so the Kronecker preconditioner dominates full AdaGrad. Regret √(2r) D Tr(L^{1/4}) Tr(R^{1/4}) = O(√T) (Theorem 7); order-k tensors use power −1/2k per dimension (Theorem 10). Steps per second within a few percent of SGD/Adam/AdaGrad (Table 1); on LM1B 3.509 against 4.87–4.92. | Read |
| [NOTE-481](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-481.md) | The Convergence Rate of Neural Networks for Learned Functions of Different Frequencies | For uniform data the wide two-layer ReLU Gram matrix H∞ is a convolution on the sphere, so its eigenvectors are Fourier modes or spherical harmonics. On S¹, bias-free: λ_k = 1/π², 1/4, 2(k²+1)/(π²(k²−1)²) and 0 for k = 0, 1, even k ≥ 2 and odd k ≥ 3 (Eq. 12). With zero-initialised bias, odd k ≥ 3 get 1/(π²k²) (Eq. 13). Learning time grows as k² on S¹ and about k³ on S², as measured for shallow, deep and residual networks (Figs. 6–7). | Read |
| [NOTE-482](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-482.md) | Gemma Scope: Open Sparse Autoencoders Everywhere All At Once on Gemma 2 | JumpReLU SAE: f(x) = z ⊙ H(z − θ), z = W_enc x + b_enc, x̂ = W_dec f + b_dec, loss ‖x − x̂‖² + λ‖f(x)‖₀, with θ trained by straight-through estimators (bandwidth ε = 0.001). Released on all layers and sublayers of Gemma 2 2B and 9B and three layers of 27B, at widths 2¹⁴–2²⁰. Evaluated by delta LM loss and FVU against L0. Residual SAEs cost the most loss; base-model SAEs transfer to the IT model; wider SAEs split latents. No agreed SAE quality metric exists. | Read |
| [NOTE-483](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-483.md) | Towards Understanding the Spectral Bias of Deep Learning | Theorem 4.2: n^(−1/2)‖V_(r_k)ᵀ(y − ŷ(T))‖ ≤ 2(1 − λ_(r_k))^T n^(−1/2)‖V_(r_k)ᵀy‖ + ε for any labels, given n ≥ Ω̃(ε^(−2) max{(λ_(r_k) − λ_(r_k+1))^(−2), M⁴r_k²}) and m ≥ Ω̃(poly(T, λ_(r_k)^(−1), ε^(−1))). Theorem 4.3: on uniform S^d the NTK's eigenfunctions are spherical harmonics, µ_k = 0 for odd k ≥ 3, and µ_k = Ω(max(k^(−d−1), d^(−k+1))) for even k. Experiments in R¹⁰ learn degrees 1, 2, 4 in that order, also on three non-uniform densities. | Read |
| [NOTE-484](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-484.md) | Bayesian hypothesis testing for psychologists: A tutorial on the Savage–Dickey method | For nested models the Bayes factor for a point null is the ratio of posterior to prior density of the tested parameter at the null value, under the larger model (Eq. 12, derived in Appendix A). It needs the nuisance parameters' prior to be continuous at the null. Worked examples give BF10 ≈ 2.2 where p ≈ .006, and BF01 ≈ 4 in favour of a null of no group difference. | Read |
| [NOTE-485](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-485.md) | An Active Inference Approach to Modeling Structure Learning: Concept Learning as an Example Case | A partially observed MDP with spare hidden-state slots learns new animal concepts without feedback, refines coarse categories into fine ones, and generalises to an unseen animal in one shot. Bayesian model reduction on the learned state prior resets unneeded slots: right in 45–80% of runs for 4–7 true concepts, rarely for 2–3, and always when the likelihood is given. | Read |
| [NOTE-486](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-486.md) | Optimizing Neural Networks with Kronecker-factored Approximate Curvature | Fisher blocks E[āāᵀ ⊗ ggᵀ] ≈ E[āāᵀ] ⊗ E[ggᵀ], with error a sum of third- and fourth-order cumulants; the inverse is approximated as block-diagonal or block-tridiagonal over layers. Factored Tikhonov damping (π chosen by trace norms), re-scaling by the exact Fisher, and a two-parameter momentum solved on the quadratic model. On three deep autoencoders it beats tuned NAG-SGD by orders of magnitude in iterations; block-tridiagonal gains 25–40% per iteration over block-diagonal. | Read |
| [NOTE-487](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-487.md) | Post hoc Bayesian model selection | Models that differ only in their priors can be scored from one fitted full model: reduced evidence is full evidence times the posterior expectation of the prior ratio. Savage–Dickey is the point-mass case, ARD the continuous one. Under Laplace the reduced free energy is closed-form. Simulations recover a sparse regression (4,096 models) and a three-edge brain network (64 models). | Read |
